A reset whose eviction is cancelled for a file (a fake file covered by a QoS requirement on that provider, which completes with `filesProcessed` above `filesEvicted` and `filesFailed` 0 and keeps its bytes) fails at its eviction stage, the message naming the provider's position, the last attempt's counts and the likely cause, and the failed stage's record carrying the counts; the same for files evicted in parallel; a gap that a later attempt clears (the first attempt evicting none) and a gap with every map empty (files the provider never held) pass; a full eviction passes with every file counted evicted; a record without the counters leaves them None and names no cause.

**O02. Replication moves whole files.** Place byte ranges by reading them through the receiving provider. *Learned:* ICSA `scripted/s2ranges.py:3-4`, `mplus.py:12-14, 144-152`. *Test:* a range placement issues range reads, never a transfer job.

**O03. Provider discovery returns every provider of the space.** Pin the agent to its declared local provider, or refuse. *Learned:* ICSA `icsa/sandbox/sandbox.py:492-506` (unreachable in campaigns only because `route.py:77-79` refuses a configuration without a named provider). *Test:* a two-provider space yields only the pinned provider; no pin raises.

**O04. Health checks take the expected providers from configuration.** *Learned:* ICSA `icsa/harness/health.py:6-7, 64-65` requires exactly one supporting provider, pinned by `tests/harness/test_campaign_glue.py:169-171`; multi-provider federations need the opposite. *Test:* a declared multi-provider support set passes; an exclusive policy rejects a second provider.

**O05. The data path is read-only, enforced fail-closed.** Mint the data path's token, and the tool-server token of a condition that declares no write right, with read-only and path caveats; mint a writing condition's tool-server token with no data-access caveat (`data.path`, `data.objectid`, `data.readonly`), since on Onedata 25.0 any of them limits a token to data access and its transfers and QoS requirements are refused, so its reach is its leased user's rights (O09); examine every token before use and refuse on any failure; enforce again at the gate and with a read-only mount. Never rely on the MCP server's token policy, which keeps write tools when the examination fails. *Learned:* ICSA's six enforcement points ([`inventory.md`](inventory.md#read-only-enforcement-in-icsa)); OMCP `onedata_mcp/token_policy.py:65-91` fails open by design; #177 (12 Nov: a writing condition's replication tool, on a token with a `data.path` caveat, was refused by `POST /transfers` with `tokenCaveatUnverified`). *Test:* a token without the read-only caveat, or an examination that raises, refuses the run; the data path's and a read-only condition's caveat lists are unchanged byte for byte, a writing condition's is its expiry and the `rest` interface, and a writing token that carries a data-access caveat is refused; no credential is visible inside the agent (environment, files, process arguments); no token the agent sends, in any header the platform reads one from, reaches the platform (O23). Live, run on 12 Nov (`deploy/compose-federation/day0/2026-11-12-federation-facts-tool-server-write.md`): (a), (b) and (d) held and (c) did not, as below. The checks: (a) a writing condition's `schedule_file_replication` replicates a file of a space it holds to a supporting provider and gets a transfer id, and the tool server's own examination of its token succeeds; (b) a writing condition's tool-server user made a member of a space outside the plane (another study's, or one the study declares for the data path alone) gets no token: the mint, or the launch of a token minted before the membership, is refused naming the space by its position or as undeclared, and no transfer is scheduled in that space; the first live run shows both reads of the user answering 200 for the harness's credentials, each with an empty list: the user's Onezone admin privileges, read with the admin credential, which must hold `oz_view_privileges` (`GET /users/{id}/effective_privileges`, `client.UNVERIFIED_ENDPOINTS`), and the clusters the user is an effective member of, read as the minted token itself (`GET /user/effective_clusters`, also in `client.UNVERIFIED_ENDPOINTS`); the same user made a member of a provider's cluster gets no token, and its launch is refused; (c) a read-only condition's tool is refused every write (a replication, an eviction, a QoS requirement), as 25.0 refuses one (400 `posix`/`eacces`, 401, 403 or `tokenCaveatUnverified`), and no transfer or requirement appears; its read tools under `/data/{id}` (file distribution, QoS summary, attributes) serve; its transfer and QoS-requirement views (`get_transfer`, `list_space_transfers`, `get_qos_requirement`) are refused today, with 401 `tokenCaveatUnverified`, as 25.0 refuses them to a token carrying `data.readonly` and `data.path`, and so is the tool server's own examination of that token, so the server keeps its write tools, which Onedata refuses: a known gap, tracked in #181, as the live run of 12 Nov measured it (`deploy/compose-federation/day0/2026-11-12-federation-facts-tool-server-write.md`); (d) the data path is unchanged: its minted caveats are the four of before, and the live contract tests and the day-0 `tokens` and `read_only` checks pass. The host keeps no copy of the token, and what is bound to hold it cannot turn into something else (13 Nov): a study's run ran `docker cp` on a running oneclient sidecar's log directory, and its teardown then raised "1 of the run's copies of the data-path token could not be removed" and quarantined the slot; the likely cause is that `docker cp` re-mounts the container's binds and `-v` makes a missing bind source a directory owned by root, which `rm -f` cannot remove (inferred). *Test:* the sidecar's and the gate's token files and the directory of the gate's TLS keys are bound with `--mount type=bind,...,readonly` and by no `-v`, and a token file that is gone when its container is created refuses the start; an empty directory at a token's path is removed with `rmdir` and recorded (`metadata()["teardown"]["token_files"]`: path in the run's directory, kind, size, owner's uid, action) without failing the teardown; a file, a link or a directory with an entry in it, or an empty one `rmdir` cannot remove, fails it with an error naming the path and the kind, and neither the error nor the record holds a content; oneclient's log goes to the run's own owner-only directory, bound at the `--log-dir` it is started with, so no collection needs `docker cp`.

**O06. Token lifetimes follow the run.** TTL is derived from the run's whole-time ceiling, the loop's `ceiling_s`, which a study may set in `[agent]` of `campaign.toml` (`ceiling_s` or `ceiling_factor`, one of them, not below the time cap; else 3 times the cap), and checked before minting; the cap alone is the agent's clock, which stops during waits, so a run can outlast it. The lifetime and the ceiling are recorded in the lease journal and the sandbox metadata, the tool server is checked against the ceiling its token was minted for, and tool-server tokens are distinct from run tokens. *Learned:* ICSA `config.py:221-234`, `tokencheck.py:20-24, 66-84`, `real_sandbox.py:73, 109` (0a2faf01, 1 Nov). *Test:* a token shorter than the ceiling plus margin is refused; a ceiling the study set is the one its tokens, journal line and tool server use, and the tool server refuses another, on the Docker and on the Apptainer backend alike (the launch is shared); a ceiling below the cap, or both keys together, is refused when `campaign.toml` is loaded.

**O07. Eventual consistency is waited out, with bounds.** Transfers are visible only once ENDED; a new space root can return ENOENT; convergence needs two consecutive matching polls. *Learned:* PPAM `fixture_runner.py:85-96, 245-288` (ae18b6e); ICSOC `harness/fixtures.py:49-64` (e4cdde3), `provision.py:368-451`, `verifier.py:475-553`; ICSA `provision.py:311-320`. *Test:* a fake federation that flips after k polls returns; one that never flips raises at the bound.

**O08. A probe that fails is recorded as failed, never guessed.** Do not retry real HTTP answers; retry network resets a bounded number of times. *Learned:* ICSOC `harness/verifier.py:39-45, 132-171, 385-418` (7ec2b62). *Test:* a 404 is one request; two resets then 200 give the value; all failing gives `probe_ok=False`, value `None`.

**O09. Mutations are scoped.** Refuse to act on a space outside the configured prefixes; refuse to evict the data provider's own copy; tear down only logged ids; a teardown that finds files left revokes nothing. A writing tool server writes only where its user has rights: its token carries no data-access caveat (O05), so its user is its boundary. Before the mint, at the tool server's launch and at its teardown, the user's rights are read in every space of the tool-server plane and must be exactly the condition's, Onezone's scope of the token (every space the user is an effective member of) must be exactly the plane's spaces, the user must hold no Onezone admin privilege, and it must be an effective member of no cluster, because a member of a provider's cluster (`cluster_update`, `cluster_delete`) can mint that provider's tokens and change or delete the provider; Onezone has no read of another user's clusters, so they are read as the token itself. *Learned:* ICSA `provision.py:33-55, 206-210, 323-333`, `harness/slot_users.py:7-13`; #177. *Test:* each refusal with a fake API; a writing condition's principal without its rights in one of the plane's spaces gets no token; a user who is a member of a space outside the plane, a data-path-only space of the study included, of a cluster, or who holds an admin privilege, gets no token, starts no tool server, sets the run aside at teardown and fails the day-0 `tokens` check; a scope, admin or clusters read that gets no answer is an outage, never a pass; a read-only condition and the data path make none of the three reads.

**O10. Destructive operations need explicit sanction.** *Learned:* ICSOC `harness/sweep.py:235`, `provider_restart.py:279-286`. *Test:* a fault injector without the sanction flag raises.

**O11. Faults and fixtures are cleared in `finally`, and clearing is verified.** "Ready" is not "restarted": confirm by a changed identity. And restarted is not ready: a restore is verified only when the provider serves (its health check healthy, its REST read answered), within a bound, before the injection is cleared and the federation released; one that does not serve in time stays open. And a restore that is not verified is never passed silently: the exit of a session raises `RestoreNotVerified` (the injector's own `injector_failure`, naming the provider's label, how the restore ended and what was checked, never a service or a token); the exit of `running`, inside which an executor keeps its record, returns, so the record itself carries the failure (the injector's reading says `restore_verified: false`, with `how`, `identity_changed` and `serving`) and the campaign never scores it, whatever the post-run health check decides; the journal keeps the injection open so the next health gate fails and the next start is refused; and a body's own exception is never masked by it (the restore failure is raised from it, or noted on an interrupt or a cancellation). And the restore always runs, whatever of the run still runs: only its heal is skipped while a step that examines the run's token may follow (O35), so no provider is left down for it. And a failed restore is tried again only once no step of the run that uses its tokens runs: while one does, the injection is handed to recovery open, its target's lock kept until the run's admission ends, the record set aside and never scored, and the next start's recovery restores it, so no second restore falls inside the token's window (O35). And a run that waits for the federation another run holds is never charged for the wait: one that runs out is the hold `federation_busy`, which costs no attempt, however often, whether the campaign's admission waited or the attempt itself did, and a health check's wait behind it, a heal included, is a deferral that charges no one even past `max_defer_s`; the injector's own failures stay `injector_failure`, charged. A restored federation is not yet one the next run can read from, so an outage run's exclusive admission outlives its sessions (`[faults] session_drain_s`, 320 s by default). During an outage an agent's read of a file only the stopped provider holds leaves an on-demand sync job on the local provider that nothing calls back, and a later read of the same file and range, from any session, joins it and hangs; it is cancelled only when the run's callers end (a REST read's request process dies, a mount's oneclient has been disconnected for about 300 s plus 15 s; op-worker f118ed9, source-read, not measured), and eviction, a transfer cancel, a delete and a link restart do not cancel it. So the federation admits no run before `session_drain_s` after the later of the run's mount disconnecting and its REST gate stopping (the sandbox records both ends and the launch reports them to the admission's drain). The wait is the remainder of one deadline, so it overlaps the restore and a heal's settle; times that are missing mean the full drain from the restore's start and never zero; the end is written to a record in the fault journal as the admission is let go, which every admission checks once it holds the lock, so it holds for the run's admission, the injector's own, the health gate's recovery and a restore that holds none (which takes the federation exclusively for the restore), and an interrupt cannot reopen it; a recovery of a crashed run's injection owes the full drain from its own start whatever the current sandbox says (a second recovery's start counts on top of the first's), and the arming comes before anything is restored, against the injections the recovery has just read, under an admission the restore keeps a share of, an admission that read the record before a lockless restore wrote it and the journal after it reads the record again and is not admitted inside the drain, and the injector's start does the same after its recovery, and waits out the drain its own recovery owes before it takes anything down; an exclusive control settles the journal as a shared run does; and only an admission the injector armed before a restore of a provider that went down writes one (a baseline run, an exclusive control and an outage run whose provider never went down write none). The injector's `drain` step records the times, the wait and why. *Learned:* ICSOC `sweep_driver.py:130-131`, `netem.py:85, 362-391` (24e2f5e), `provider_restart.py:87-104` (f627555); a study's wiring that caught only a raising exit passed a failed restore (`clear()` False, the snapshot's `ok` false) silently (13 Nov 2026); a wait that ran out was `injector_failure`, so with the heal of O35 holding the federation for over an hour, time-outs inside attempts would have exhausted the runs waiting behind it (14 Nov 2026); and the source of op-worker f118ed9 (14 Nov 2026) shows that the reads of an outage run leave on-demand sync jobs that a later read of the same file and range would join and hang on, until the run's callers end. *Test:* a wait that runs out, shared or exclusive, is `federation_busy`, uncharged and untreatable; an injector whose own wait runs out raises it and takes nothing down; a wait that runs out inside an outage run's session drain is the same hold, whichever way it waits, the injector's own after its journal read included; four in-attempt waits that run out, more than `max_attempts`, leave the run uncharged and then done, while an injector that cannot take its target down is charged and exhausts the run; a campaign run waiting behind an outage run's heal is refused only as the hold, never charged with `max_attempts` of 1, and runs once the heal lets the federation go; a health check whose wait runs out during a heal is deferred and charges no one, and after the heal failed finds the failed restore; a pre-run check deferred past `max_defer_s` behind a live holder closes the gate blocking and charges no run; a raising trial still calls clear and teardown; a driver whose provider never serves again makes the exit of `session` raise `RestoreNotVerified` (kind `injector_failure`, the label and the word `not_serving`, no service name) and the exit of `running` return, both with the injection open and journalled `clear_failed` and the federation still held, the next health gate fails for it by the label and another injector's start is refused, the reading in the record says `restore_verified` false with the three fields (and true for a verified restore, and nothing for an injector that took nothing down), a record that carries it is set aside and never re-scored, and the scheduler sets its attempt aside as `injector_failure` and runs it again, though the post-run health check passes and with or without an instrument check of its own (a scored treatment attempt included, and an attempt whose restore was verified is scored as before), a body exception plus a failed restore surfaces both (the body's as the failure's cause), an interrupt or a cancellation, in the body or during the clear itself, is raised as it was with the failure in its notes (or a note that the clear was still running when the bounded wait for it ended), a start that failed and cannot restore raises the restore failure from the start's, the instrument route refuses the attempt through `check` (`finish_instruments` records the refusal and keeps the snapshot), and a verified restore leaves every exit as it was; the drain (`tests/onedata/test_fault_drain.py`, `tests/onedata/sandbox/test_session_ends.py`, `tests/onedata/sandbox/test_fault_launch.py`): an armed exclusive admission is let go at once into a federation that refuses a bounded try for the lock, shared and exclusive, until the drain has ended and grants it after, an `acquire` waits for the record inside its bound (`FederationBusy` past it) and a cancelled wait lets its lock go, a record that cannot be written is waited out in process, an interrupted wait keeps the lock for the life of the process, one that cannot be read refuses every admission, the record only moves later, and the wall clock is read after the monotonic one so that a pause between them never publishes an earlier deadline; the drain runs from the later of the mount's end and the gate's stop, waits only the remainder after a restore of 180 s, waits nothing once it is over, and waits the full 320 s from the restore's start when no sandbox reported and when a mount never reported its end (even with the gate's known), never earlier than a known end plus it, a sleep that comes back early is slept again until the deadline and a clock that does not move cannot hang it; a recovery owes the full drain from its start whatever the current run's sandbox, which has no data path, says, by the health gate (the federation is closed and its own check deferred), by a start under an admission, and by `recover()` with none (which takes the federation for the restore, and fails and restores nothing when it cannot); a sandbox with no data path and `session_drain_s = 0` wait nothing; the injector's own admission is kept until the drain is over and the restore's time counts (320 s from the end of the sessions, not 500), stays closed to a shared try at every sleep of the drain, and its snapshot says the times, the wait and why; a run's admission closes the federation at its end, not at the restore (the step says `deferred`), including when the sandbox ended before the restore, for what is left of the drain; a provider the stop never took down and a restore that is not verified drain nothing, and the clear that later verifies it does; a baseline run and an exclusive control write no record and are let go at once, and the next run is admitted at once when none is pending; `cancel_wait_s` counts the drain; the sandbox records the gate's stop at the collection and the mount's end when its sidecar is gone, and none for a teardown that could not release the mount; the launch reports both to the drain of the exclusive admission, and a stop that failed leaves the end unknown; an outage run's exclusive hold of the federation overlaps no other run's hold, compared pairwise from each hold's entry and exit, the readers on the federation at its arrival finish before it is admitted, and readers arriving while it waits are admitted after it. Every party holds until the test lets it go, so the contention never rests on a sleep, and each arrival is sequenced on the lock's own state (a reader in, the outage run holding the turnstile), never on how soon a thread starts. The deciding checks are bounded tries for the lock made by the test thread itself, which rest on no other thread's progress and so cannot pass because a thread is slow or paused: with the readers present and before the outage run starts, an exclusive request is refused (it times out, and lets the turnstile go); once the outage run holds the turnstile, with the present readers still holding shared, a shared request is refused, and it is refused again while the outage run holds the lock. A waiting party shown not in after a grace is a secondary check only, since it also passes for a thread that has not got as far as the lock. An exclusive admission downgraded to shared, one downgraded whose thread pauses right after taking the turnstile, a turnstile that queues nobody and a turnstile let go before the main lock each fail it. The tool server's check threads (`tests/onedata/test_toolserver_admission.py`) hold a share of the run's admission while they run: a launch cancelled while the check before the spawn or the check at teardown reads Onezone leaves the federation held until that thread has ended (the test parks the thread on an event, cancels the launch, leaves the admission and tries for the lock exclusively, refused until the event is set and then granted), a check that raises and a launch that runs its course give their shares up, and a task cancelled before its thread began has its share given up for it; a check thread keeps its count as the tool server's step and its share for its whole life, giving both up when it ends, and one whose share cannot be taken (the run's shortcut section has sealed the admission) is refused before it starts, holding nothing. **The reset lock (`federation.reset`, `resetlock.py`, `resetgrant.py`).** A shortcut reset (O51) skips the block maps of files a planner's reading showed empty, which is sound only while no reader places bytes, and an on-demand read leaves no transfer record. So every shared admission (a campaign run's, a tool's outside a campaign, the health check's) holds `federation.reset` shared beside `federation.lock`, taken after it and outside the turnstile, recorded in `reset-holds.jsonl` (a hold that cannot be recorded is refused), and every reset, full or shortcut, takes it exclusively (O66): its run gives up its own share (its agent has not started) and never its share of `federation.lock`, waits within `admission_timeout_s` (past it, refused as `ResetLockBusy`, uncharged, naming the holders by process, a dead one `holder_gone`, nothing sent; it used to do the full reads beside them, recorded `shortcut_unavailable`), and afterwards takes its share back within the same bound (past it, `FederationBusy`, and the run's infrastructure stays refused). A flock is never converted in place: each mode is a new descriptor. The lock order is `federation.intent`, `federation.lock`, `federation.reset`; no holder of a later lock waits for an earlier one, and a resetter waits holding no reset share, so two resetters never deadlock, an outage run never gets in during a reset (the run's `federation.lock` share never lapses), and no other run's agent, tool or health check acts during one (each acts only under its share; the resetting run's own infrastructure is refused while its share is given up). No lock outlives its holder: lock files are opened `O_CLOEXEC`, held locks are closed in a forked child, no `subprocess` call passes descriptors, and the tool server, which the MCP client starts in a session of its own, holds none; `PR_SET_PDEATHSIG` is not set (the client gives no hook, and it would reach only a launcher's process and fire with the forking thread), and instead a section that finds a holder whose process is gone takes no shortcut, and neither does any for `DEAD_HOLDER_QUIET_S` (3,600 s) after, since a read it sent, or a tool server it started, may still place bytes. The section seals the run's admission, granted only while no worker of the run holds a share (`shares_retained` otherwise), so no worker takes one and nothing of the run acts until it ends; a health check in the run's own context and a sandbox's start (`retain_to_act`, from its admission check until its start thread holds its own share) hold a share while they act, so no section begins under them, and an interrupt before the grant exists lets the exclusive hold go and takes the share back; and a section stands for its own federation only. *Test (`tests/onedata/test_reset_exclusive.py`):* a run's shared admission holds the reset lock and records it, an exclusive one takes none; a concurrent shared run holds the shortcut off until it releases; a tool's hold that ends within the bound lets it in, and one past the bound makes it unavailable, naming the live holder by pid, boot id and start time and no reason, the run's share back; a dead holder beside a live one is shown `holder_gone`; a granted section that finds a dead holder gives no shortcut and ends its line, and the next sections refuse it too until the quiet window has passed, while a live process's unrecorded end refuses nothing; a worker holding a share keeps the shortcut off with nothing given up, and none takes a share during a section; a nested section for another federation is refused and a reading of another federation's section gives the full reads; a health check in the resetting run's own context is deferred, and one in a run's context, or a launch while it mints, holds a share that keeps a section from sealing the admission; a start is refused while the section seals it or the share is given up; an interrupt while the section reads its holders leaves the lock free of the exclusive hold and the share back; two resetters never deadlock and never hold the lock together; an outage run is refused during a shortcut reset; a watcher that keeps trying `federation.lock` exclusively never gets it through the whole section; the run's infrastructure and a reusing tool are refused while its share is given up; a health check is deferred, uncharged, and passes after; a run that holds the federation exclusively is granted at once; the shortcut is off by default and unavailable without an admission or `[faults]`; the share is taken back when the body raises, and one that another reset keeps past the bound, or whose line cannot be written, refuses the agent; a campaign process killed with SIGKILL in its agent phase or inside its section, with a child started as the tool server is and still alive, leaves both locks free; a forked child keeps no copy of the share; no lock descriptor is inheritable and a child started with `close_fds=False` holds none; no call of the package passes descriptors; and a share that cannot be recorded refuses the admission, both locks free. **A baseline before the stop (`FaultInjector.prepared`).** A study's probes read their baseline after the injector's admission, its recovery of what an earlier run left open, the drain that recovery owes and the drain record, and before the target's lock, journal entry and stop: `with injector.prepared(identity) as prepared: <baseline>; prepared.inject()`, of which `start` is the block with nothing in it but `inject`. A block left without a stop lets its admission go and a stop keeps it until the restore, and the block never restores. Its window is unbounded, so the injector's lock is not held across the body: what would interleave is refused at once or, for a clear, cancels the block, and `inject` re-reads what the window could have made stale. *Test (`tests/onedata/test_fault_prepared.py`):* `start` and a block whose body only injects journal the same lines, record the same snapshot, make the same driver calls and read the injector's clock and sleep alike (the block alone re-reads, one wall-clock reading and the link-epoch record it makes), and `start` matches `start` as it was before the block, rebuilt from its two unchanged halves, down to every clock read and the files it makes, over a start that recovers a crashed run's injection, waits out the drain it owes and then a drain record that outlasts it; a probe started with `start_instruments` inside a block, in the worker thread of an exclusively admitted run, reads the earlier injection recovered, its drain waited out, the federation held exclusively, the drain record passed and the target up with nothing stopped, and finishes before the restore with the target down; an exception, an interrupt, a body that never injects and an inject that fails before its stop each give the federation back with nothing journalled or stopped and the target's lock free, and a block that took the target down keeps the federation held until `finish`, whether its body then returns or raises; a second block, a start and a recovery inside a block are refused as `RuntimeError` and leave it whole, and so are a second inject and an inject outside its block; a start, a block, a block built before the first one opened and entered only now, and a recovery from another thread while the block's inject holds the lock in its stop are refused at once, not after waiting for it, and a clear meanwhile waits for the inject and restores the target; a clear from another thread while the baseline runs returns at once, a start from it is refused at once, and the block's later inject refuses as `injector_failure` with nothing stopped; and inject refuses, touching nothing, after a drain record raised during the baseline, a link epoch raised, spoiled (with no record before the block, which then makes one), made anew or unreadable during it or unreadable already at the preparation, a link check under way that it waits for and that then raises the epoch, and one that outlasts its wait (each `federation_busy`), while a plain start is held back by no link check and makes no record, an injection journalled by a second injector of the same run, and a call from a context without the run's admission.

**O12. Federation names live in configuration only.** *Learned:* ICSA hard-codes them in at least six modules (`day0/scripts/od.py:26-34`, `harness/real_sandbox.py:27-34`, `slot_users.py:29-33`, `scripted/federation.py:21-41`, `knowledge/launch.py:21-22`, `knowledge/live_equality.py:26-29`), and `campaign/boverride.py` (845 lines) exists only to patch them in memory (d867010d, 4 Nov). *Test:* the hashed study-token test over the platform layer.

**O13. Sandbox images are pinned by resolved id.** *Learned:* ICSA `sandbox.py:283-288` (7c70f8f0, 1 Nov). *Test:* a moved tag refuses start.

**O14. Each sandbox gets its own small network from a dedicated pool.** *Learned:* ICSA `icsa/sandbox/netpool.py:3-39` (Docker's default pools ran out at concurrency 16). *Test:* 16 concurrent creates get disjoint networks.

**O15. FUSE teardown is ordered.** Readers, then unmount, then sidecar, then agent; the host froze otherwise. *Learned:* ICSA `icsa/sandbox/teardown.py:1-30` (f1c242a8, 3 Nov). *Test:* the teardown order.

**O16. The data gate makes no errors of its own and marks cut transfers.** *Learned:* ICSA `icsa/sandbox/gate.py:23-32` (1761261d, 1 Nov; e43b9f46, 3 Nov), `gatepolicy.py:15-18, 60-75`. *Test:* concurrent reads give no gate-origin 5xx; a client closing mid-body yields a close line with bytes unread.

**O17. Kill causes come from the kernel, never the exit code.** *Learned:* ICSA `icsa/sandbox/killcause.py:1-34`, `infradeath.py:1-42`, `earlyoom.py:1-14`. *Test:* each cause fixture maps to its category; unknown is retried within the attempt limit. **The run's other infrastructure, read by one name on both backends:** `infra_check()` (`api.InfraReadings`) gives on Docker exactly what `infra_container_check()` gave, which still answers and warns (`DeprecationWarning`); on an HPC node it reads the collector, the gate, the mount's oneclient with its mount and the guard's watcher by role, in Docker's shape: a process that ended, one whose state cannot be read and a mount gone from its mount point are each a death of `undetermined` infrastructure, and a stop of the harness's own (the collection's, the guard window's close, the teardown's) none, the component judged by its state just before, a stop that lands during the check's own read included; the collection's deaths come from the same check, so a dead oneclient or watcher is in them. *Test (both):* the Docker check and its old name agree, healthy and with a dead gate; on a node a healthy REST and mount run read clean before the collection, after it and after the stop, and a gate, a collector, a oneclient, a mount or a watcher that ended, or a state the node cannot tell, is a death before the collection, in its deaths and after it.

**O18. Errors the agent caused are not platform errors.** *Learned:* ICSA `icsa/sandbox/oneclient_log.py:30-45` (93fe68df, 1 Nov). *Test:* ENOENT, EROFS and EISDIR on a client log line are not sidecar errors. `mount_errors()` and `mount_log()` read oneclient's own log on both backends, the token replaced by `<token>`: on Docker the same `docker exec` and summary as `sidecar_errors()`, which still answers and warns; on an HPC node the host mount's log directory. *Test (both):* the summary and the text of the same read, the token gone; a log that cannot be read is `unavailable` by every name, never an empty count; a run without a mount has neither reading and asks nothing.

**O19. Collecting a run's output never follows or keeps a link.** What the agent left is copied out as root only after a root listing that follows nothing shows each output, and every directory on the way to it, to hold regular files and directories alone. A symbolic link, a hard link to a file outside the output, a FIFO, socket or device, another file system, a path too long to copy, or more bytes than the working disk holds is refused before anything is copied. The agent made each of these, so it is the agent's own doing: none of the outputs is copied, the collection names each object by its relative path and what it is, never a link's target, under `refused_output` in its result and the run's metadata for the study's scorers, and the attempt's outcome is the loop's and the scorers', never set aside, which would hide the behaviour and reward it with a fresh attempt. A FIFO, socket, device or directory of another file system on the way to an output is refused the same way. The agent's container is frozen through its cgroup, confirmed by the cgroup's own state and never by a list of processes, while its outputs are listed and copied, and a root helper copies name by name from each parent directory's descriptor with `O_NOFOLLOW`, checking every object against the listing, so whatever of the agent's survived the kills cannot change the tree, and a name swapped after the listing is never read through. The copy is verified to hold only directories and files of one link, the driver's own. A collection that fails for the harness's own reasons (a container that cannot be frozen, a copy command or the disk failing, a copy that is not what the listing showed, a gate log that is not a plain file) sets the attempt aside as infrastructure of a kind no condition may declare (`output_collection`). The driver reads the gate log without following a link, and the reaper's copy of a dead run's output follows the same rule, keeping its disks when it refuses. *Learned:* #129 (the first study's live-environment lane: `cp -r` and `chown -R` under sudo over output the agent controls, so a link it left still pointed into the host from the copy that records and packages are built from; its reviews: an object the agent made is its outcome, not infrastructure, and a process that survived the kills could swap a directory between the listing and a copy by path). *Test:* a link to a host file, to another directory, on the way to an output and dangling; a hard link out of the output; a FIFO, a socket and devices; a sparse file bigger than the disk and a path too long are each refused and named for the scorers, with nothing outside the output copied, changed or readable through the copy and no set-aside; a deep tree and names with newlines or leading dashes are copied exactly; a failed copy command and a copy that holds a link after all are infrastructure, set aside even where `sandbox` is the treatment; the listing and the copy happen while the container is frozen, and a pause the cgroup does not confirm copies nothing; a directory swapped after the listing, for a link or another directory, is never read through; a FIFO, a device or a mount on the way to an output is refused as the agent's doing; a gate log replaced by a link is neither copied nor read; the reaper refuses a link or a FIFO and keeps the disks.

**O20. A read-only input is read-only in the agent's container and named by its hash in the record.** Files a study supplies to the agent, such as scripts it may run but not change, are copied into the run's directory, hashed while they are copied, and mounted read-only in the agent's container from that copy, so the bytes hashed are the bytes the agent sees. The start proves from inside the agent that writing, renaming, chmod-ing and deleting a file of the input and creating a file or a directory beside it each fail with EROFS, which no permission the agent could change gives, that the agent can read the input and run its executable files, and that its working directory stays writable; it then hashes the copy and the source again and refuses an input that changed between its hash and its mount. The hash, with the input's path in the agent, its files and its bytes, is in the run's sandbox metadata (`read_only_inputs`) and in the run's record itself (`InputsRecord`, `RunRecord.instruments["read_only_inputs"]`), so a changed input changes the record. The plugin declares its inputs (`inputs.declare`), checked against every path the sandbox reserves and hashed, and puts them in the campaign fingerprint, so a resume with changed inputs is refused; `launch` refuses an input it was not declared with, and every start refuses one whose content left its declared hash. The run's copy is root's and read-only on the host too, and the collection hashes it again. *Learned:* #130 (the first study's live-environment lane: its preregistration delivers its analysis scripts read-only and codes an agent that edits them as a failure cause, and the sandbox had no way to deliver a read-only input). *Test:* the agent's container mounts the copy with `readonly`; a probe answer that shows a change succeeding or refused by permissions alone, the input unreadable, its script not executable or `/work` not writable refuses the start; the probe run for real against a directory only its modes protect is refused; the hash follows the content, the names and the executable bit; an input changed after its hash refuses the start, and a changed script refuses a campaign's resume and every later start; `launch` refuses an undeclared input and one with no record; the hash reaches `RunRecord.instruments`; declaring an input at a reserved path is refused; the copy is root's, 0444 and 0555, and a copy changed before collection sets the run aside; a path in the agent that is, holds or lies under another mount or input, and a source holding a link, a special file or no file, are refused before anything exists; against a real docker daemon the agent runs and reads the script, every change fails with EROFS and `/work` stays writable.

**O21. A complete listing of an unchanging directory is complete or refused, never a part taken for the whole.** In a REST data path's complete listing mode the gate answers a listing with every entry of the directory, merged from the pages it follows upstream; it goes on while a page says it is not the last, however short; a page that neither ends nor continues the listing, a page with a key given twice, a token or an entry given twice, or a page after the first that is not a 200 is the platform's 502; a listing whose entries and tokens pass the gate's bound is refused for its integrity (`listing_too_large`, the run set aside), and the gate keeps only a fixed-size digest of each token and entry; a listing that names where it starts is refused. The mode is selected per level of the data path and recorded in the run's metadata and in the campaign fingerprint. Onedata gives a listing no snapshot, so this holds only for a directory that does not change while it is read: an entry inserted meanwhile shows as an entry given twice and is refused, but one removed meanwhile can shift another out unseen, a known limit. *Learned:* ICSA's merged listing refused a page with no valid end rather than rewrite it as complete, but had no bound and took no other page for a failure (`icsa/sandbox/gate.py:846-888`, `gatepolicy.py:14, 51, 74-75`); the official client's `ls` reads one page only and the API says a short page can still not be the last (`day0/evidence/docs_research.md:24-47, 151-166`); the core did not carry the mode over until the first consumer's pilot needed it (#92, the triage on #28). *Test:* a directory of 2,500 entries comes back whole and in order through a complete gate and as one page through a paged one; each malformed or failing later page is a 502 with no entry passed, a page with `isLast` twice and an entry shifted into the next page by an insertion among them; the byte and page bounds are `listing_too_large`, and empty pages with long tokens reach the byte bound; a walk keeps 16 bytes of each token; `token`, `index` and `offset` are refused; a mode on a mount, or one the gate does not know, refuses the load, the start or the gate; a resume after a level changed its listing mode is refused.

**O22. The bytes a run's agent read through its data path are counted, and unknown is never zero.** Count them once, from the log of the gate the collection stopped: the body bytes of the platform's answers that the gate's socket accepted for the agent, split into file content, listings as served and other answers, with the gate's own answers apart. Count them per file whose content was served and per directory listed as well: by the id the request's path names, the answers, the unique byte ranges served (where the platform's answer says they begin, never where the agent asked) and the body bytes, in tables bounded by caps platform.toml declares, which the snapshot records and which say when they cut a table; a file or byte a cap left out reads as unknown, never as not served or 0 bytes. Record the count per attempt through the platform's instrument (`served_bytes`), so a scorer reads it from the record and replay serves it. A mount's reads, which no gate sees, are unknown; a run with no data path read zero; a log that may lack a line (the gate not shown stopped, no `teardown_complete` mark from the gate whatever its exit code, a request in flight when it stopped, a close line or a log write it could not make, its own output not read, a line that cannot be read) makes every count unknown, and every table with it. *Learned:* ICSA's gate logged each answer's body bytes, never fewer than the client received (`icsa/sandbox/gate.py:503-512`), but the run's summary added only the content bytes (`sandbox.py:100-112`); re-expressing ICSA on the core found no documented per-run count of the bytes the agent read (#73, #92). The first consumer checks its agents' claimed reads against what the gate served, and a per-run total cannot catch a read log that moves reads between files (#92). *Test:* the count from a log adds each answer by its kind; each way a line may be missing makes every count and every table unknown with its reason, an exit without the gate's mark, an output `docker logs` could not read and a content or listing line that names no file or directory among them; the gate writes its mark after every close line and none when one fails; through a real gate the count equals the bytes the client received, in the complete and the paged listing modes, and each file's ranges start where the platform's `Content-Range` says; two files read in overlapping ranges keep their own merged ranges; a file read again counts each request and its bytes once in its ranges; a file only looked at has no row; a range the log cannot place leaves that file's ranges unknown; each cap cuts its table and says so; a run past its declared file cap reads a file left out as cut, never 0, and the same run with a raised cap lists every file; a byte past a cut row's last range is unknown and one in a gap below it is not served; a cap that is not an integer of at least 1 is refused at load; every snapshot records the caps in effect; a REST run's count is unknown before its collection and fixed by it; a mount is unknown and no data path is zero, with empty tables; the instrument puts the count in the record.

**O23. The published client works on a REST data path with a placeholder token, and no token the agent sends reaches the platform.** The agent's environment holds `ONEDATA_TOKEN` set to a configured placeholder, never a credential, and a placeholder equal to the run's token refuses the start. The agent image's certifi bundle is extended with the run's CA, since the client verifies against it and reads none of the CA variables; an image without certifi starts and its metadata says so, and an image that cannot say where its bundle is refuses the start. The gate drops every header in which the platform reads a token, the deprecated `Macaroon` and the consumer and service tokens included, and replaces the token in a scope inference's body, which then goes to the zone with no token in any header (O30), and it answers 400 to a request whose header block folds a line (one that starts with a space or a tab) or holds a CR or LF inside one, before anything is forwarded, since the upstream would read the folded line as a header of its own. *Learned:* the published fsspec client raises at init unless `ONEDATA_TOKEN` is set, and the sandbox gave the REST agent none (#150, from the first study's agent-image work); the client sends through `requests.Session.send`, which merges no environment setting, so the sandbox's CA variables never reached it (measured against the real gate on #150); the gate forwarded `Macaroon`, `X-Onedata-Consumer-Token` and `X-Onedata-Service-Token`, which ctool's `src/auth/tokens.erl` and `include/http/headers.hrl` parse. *Test:* with only the agent's environment and the default trust, the pinned published client lists a directory and reads a file whole through the gate before a fake platform that answers only the run's token, failing at init without the placeholder and at certificate verification without the certifi bundle; each token header, in any case, repeated or with white space before the colon, and a scope body naming the placeholder or a guessed token, reach the upstream as the gate's token only; the placeholder is configurable and refused unless it can be a header value; the certifi question runs as the agent with no network, its answer `none` starts without a bundle, and any other answer refuses the start with nothing left; the live test sends the same code to a real REST agent.

**O24. A harness step before the agent's loop is kept apart from the agent's evidence.** A check the harness runs in the sandbox before the agent's loop, as the agent's user, is recorded as a harness step with its exit status and the host killer's kills it met, never as a cell of the agent's; its gate refusals and its kills are never put on the agent's first cell. A step runs only before the agent's run: after the mark its requests would count as the agent's, and between the agent's cells it would take their refusals and kills. The executor then marks where the agent's run begins: the gate writes the mark in its own log, naming every request it had begun and not yet logged, and the sandbox reads the mark back before the loop starts. An agent's cell has begun once its `exec` is entered, so a mark during the agent's first cell, which would set that cell's reads apart while the count reads as known, is refused, as is a mark while a step runs. The bytes served, their tables and the lookups that read them take only the answers after the mark; those before it are counted apart, so nothing is hidden. A run with no mark counts everything, and its snapshot says no mark was set; a mark the sandbox did not read back, or that the log does not show once and before the gate's teardown mark, makes every count unknown, never everything after taken for the run. *Learned:* the first consumer's executor checks its data path as the agent's user before the loop, so that a misconfigured path is set aside as infrastructure, and both the check's cell and its requests landed in the run's evidence, where its forged-read-log check compares the gate's count with the reads the agent claims (#152). *Test:* a harness step leaves no agent cell and keeps its exit status, its refusal, its memory kill and its host killer's kill are not the agent's first cell's, and a step after the mark or after an agent's cell has begun is refused; a mark during the agent's first cell or during a step is refused; answers before the mark, and one the mark names as begun before it, are counted apart and in no table, and a lookup of what only the harness read is not served; no mark counts everything, with the flag; a mark line lost, cut, not read back, given twice or after the teardown mark makes the count unknown, and a mark nobody set or one without its requests is malformed; the gate names each request begun and not yet logged and handles its signal before it says it is ready; the mark is refused twice, after an agent's cell and after the collection; through a real gate, a mark sent as soon as the harness's answer arrived keeps that answer apart.

**O25. A large file moves in bounded chunks, resumes where it stopped, and counts as moved only when its hash matches.** Upload from a local path a chunk at a time, 16 MiB by default, each chunk a write at its offset through the mutation gate bound to the provisioner's space, with an explicit content type (`application/octet-stream` by default), and send each write of at least `[placement] fresh_connection_bytes` (16 MiB by default, so every full chunk) on a connection of its own, never one the pool kept: a client that owns its pool uses a one-shot client built as the pool's is. Skip a target that already holds the same size and SHA-256; resume a target this provisioner created that holds a strict prefix, by offset writes from its end; write any other target it created that is no longer than the local file again from its first byte, every write at its offset, and refuse one longer than it (only a write without an offset could shorten it, and none is sent, O44); refuse a differing file it did not create. Read a file back by ranges, each gated as a range read and streamed, hashing as the bytes arrive, bounded by the range and a deadline. Report an upload only once the size and the SHA-256 read back equal those of the bytes sent. *Learned:* the first study's ingest of a 2.17 GB table (#127): `put_file` took the file as bytes in memory, sent no content type (Onedata 25.0 answers 415 without one) and refused a file already there, so an interrupted ingest could not resume, and nothing read a file back over REST without holding it. The API description says a write with `offset` keeps what lies beyond and one without truncates. #186, from a 200 MiB upload on the 25.0 development build (`deploy/compose-federation/day0/2026-11-12-federation-facts-upload-and-stalls.md`): chunks of 64 and 32 MiB stalled on reused keep-alive connections (3 of 289 reused PUTs, none of 336 on new ones), the body started and never finished and the provider closed the connection at about 300 s; 16 MiB and every smaller size passed every run, reused or not, and with keep-alive off every size passed. Each write of content is logged with how its connection began, so a stall is set against it. *Test:* a file of 64 chunks goes in with no local read and no write larger than a chunk; a second upload sends nothing, and a file another made with the same bytes is skipped and stays out of the log; an upload cut by a reset, or cut inside a chunk, resumes from where the target ends; a changed target, or a shorter one with other bytes, is written again from its first byte with every write at its offset, and a longer one, an empty local file over it included, is refused with nothing written; a differing file the log does not hold is refused with nothing written; content the platform stores otherwise fails the upload; every write of `put_file` and an upload carries `application/octet-stream` unless another type is named, which the fake, like its 25.0 shape, answers 415; the read keeps its memory flat over 32 MiB, refuses an answer that is not exactly its range, more or fewer bytes, a changed size and a passed deadline, sends no range for a file outside the prefixes or, from a provisioner bound to one space, for a file of another under the same prefix, waits until a provider other than the data provider sees the file, keeps a deleted file's 400 `posix` `enoent` so it reads as absent, and leaves nothing at its destination when it fails; a gated live test resumes, skips, refuses a shorter file over the longer target and reads back a 40 MB file; the default chunk is 16 MiB and the default threshold for a new connection is 16 MiB, as the dataclass has them and as the loader reads them from a table that names neither; against a real socket and the real pool, with the threshold at the chunk size, each full chunk is the only request on a connection the server accepted for it, built by the layer's own client builder with the pool's configuration, and the remainder, below it, goes on the pool's connection that carried earlier requests; with the threshold one byte above the chunk size every request of the upload shares one connection and no one-shot client is built; each write is logged with its size and whether its connection was new, with no path, id or credential; an upload cut by a reset resumes from the prefix and its resumed chunks are each on a connection of their own; a client given an HTTP client by its caller builds none; a 50 MiB upload at the defaults, no setting changed, is three full chunks each on a connection of their own and a remainder of 2 MiB, read back with the SHA-256 of the bytes; and a gated live test uploads 50 MiB at the default chunk size, with no override, reads it back by its hash and records each PUT's connection. *Learned (post-merge review of #196):* the threshold was inserted among the settings before `probe_attempts`, so a caller passing `PlacementSettings` positionally had every later argument shift by one and a probe failed with a `TypeError` (`probe_attempts` a float); a field added to the settings goes last, and a call with the order from before #196 gives the fields it always did. *From an archive's member:* an upload's source (and `write_range`'s) may be a `streamed.Slice`, `size` bytes of a local file from its byte `offset` read as a file of its own: no read of the file reaches before the offset or past the range's end, whatever the `stop`; a skip, a resume or a restart compares the target with the slice's own bytes, never the file's from its first byte; and a slice whose file does not hold its whole range is refused before anything is sent. `streamed.tar_members` gives each member of an uncompressed tar as such a slice and refuses the archive whole when it is compressed or no tar, or a member is sparse, a link, not a regular file, a name held twice, not a plain path relative to a space's root, has a pax size that is no plain count, or reaches past the archive's end, when bytes up to where the reading stopped belong to no member returned or a global pax header, and when the archive does not end in its end-of-archive blocks there (`tarfile` reads a pax size it cannot parse as 0 and ends its reading quietly at a damaged later header, or after one it failed inside, dropping members without a word). *Test (archives):* a slice's chunks are its bytes at its own offsets, and every read of the file stays inside the range for a stop at, one past and far past its end; ustar, pax and GNU archives give each member, a long name and an empty one included, as the range holding its bytes; each refusal, five compressions among them and a plain tar whose first name begins like one read as a tar; a member uploaded, skipped, cut by a reset and resumed from its own bytes (an archive whose first bytes are a header and another member, so a resume reading those fails), uploaded to a stop and resumed, written again over a differing target, and written by range; a slice past its file's end refused with nothing sent; a damaged checksum and a header cut short after the first member, an archive missing one or both end blocks and one with data after them, pax sizes that are no count, a member tarfile drops after a pax size of -6, and any `GNU.sparse.` pax key, each refused; a global pax header read past, its keys checked on each member.

**O26. A listing without its last-page marker is refused, never taken as complete.** A children page ends the listing only with `isLast` true; a page without a boolean `isLast`, or with `isLast` false and no next-page token, is refused. A page of the transfer listing ends it only with `nextPageToken` null; a page without the key, or with one that is neither null nor a token, is refused. Every reader of a listing in the platform layer applies the rule: the provisioner's, the pre-campaign check's, the day-0 listing check's and the probes' transfer log, and so does the Compose federation's baseline writer. *Learned:* a Codex finding of the first study's own ingest code (#127); the core's three children readers took a page without `isLast` for the last one, and the transfer log a page without `nextPageToken`. The API says a page can be shorter than its limit and still not be the last, that `isLast` is the only valid sign that every entry has been listed, and that the transfer listing's token is null at its end (ICSA `day0/evidence/docs_research.md:44-46`, the provider's API description at 25.0); a real 25.0 provider answered `isLast` as a boolean on every page the earlier study listed (`day0/evidence/listing_rest_children.json`). The Compose federation measured how each listing ends on 25.0 (`deploy/compose-federation/day0/2026-11-12-federation-facts-listing-end.md`): a children page always carries `nextPageToken`, `isLast` and `children`, its token a non-empty string on every page, the last included, so the end is `isLast` true alone; a page the last entries fill is followed by an empty page with `isLast` true, and the page after a last page answers 400 `posix` `eagain`; a transfer page carries `transfers` and `nextPageToken` and no `isLast`, its token null on the last page, and a full last page is followed by an empty one with a null token. *Test:* a children page without either marker, without `isLast`, or with one that is a string or null is refused, and so is a page not the last without a token, while a listing of many short pages is read whole; a last page that carries a token, and the empty last page after a full one, end the listing; a directory whose entries fill its pages exactly is read whole by the provisioner, the pre-campaign check and the day-0 listing check, none asking for the page after the last; the fakes answer both listings in the measured shapes, the page after a last page included; a path on a page after a cut one is not reported missing; the pre-campaign check does not call its file absent, and the day-0 check fails instead of passing on two cut reads that agree; a transfer page without `nextPageToken`, or with an empty one, is unreadable and the probes record the listing as unknown, while one without `isLast` is read; the federation's baseline writer lists a directory to its last page and refuses a page without its marker.

**O27. Data that arrives during a run arrives on its schedule, verified, every file id recorded, and the dataset's baseline comes back without losing what the study keeps.** A progressive ingest writes each part of a planned file when it falls due, through the provisioner bound to the study's space, and counts it only once the hash read back is the plan's hash of that prefix, the data provider's block map covers it and every provider that must see the file shows it at its size. Each step is a clock-labelled reading (planned, written, confirmed), and every file id the ingest removes, creates or restores is recorded with the file's label and the old and new id, in the snapshot and in a journal kept across runs. The start restores the baseline first, which recovers a crashed run, then removes each file whose baseline is complete, confirmed absent on every provider before its name is written again; the finish restores the baseline, verified, each file on its own so that one which fails leaves no other missing. A file at a planned path is removed only when the ingest made it or its bytes are its staged source's, and every removal checks that staged source's hash first, the ingest's own files included, in an "absent" file's restore as in a "complete" one's and in the start's removals; a damaged copy leaves the file in place and fails that file's restore alone (fail closed: a failed restore is infrastructure, which is recoverable, and a silent loss of data is not); anything else is refused with nothing sent. A start state that cannot be set, a part that cannot be written or confirmed, and a restore that fails are infrastructure of the non-treatable kind `ingest_failure`, and the parts after a failure are recorded as abandoned, never skipped in silence; the instrument's post-run check (C31) refuses such an attempt, so the scheduler sets it aside as charged infrastructure whatever the executor does, and it is never scored. A part not due when the run ends is the condition's manipulation, recorded as not due. A file id is recorded as soon as the file exists, before any check, and a file an upload made before it failed is recorded as created. The ingest's change log is its own: one another user wrote is refused, and a teardown over it sends nothing. A snapshot binds to the plan that made it by the plan's content, never by the files' labels (#262): `ingestbind.check_recorded_plan` compares the plan's hash, the mode, each file's size, source, schedule and prefix and every reading's part and due time, refuses any difference naming the file and the field, and refuses a snapshot that records no plan hash, a file's field or its steps, since unknown is not a pass. *Learned:* #107, from the first federation study's need for a "data still arriving" state. Its design review found that the reset as first specified would delete 14 days of a dataset's record at every run, files every other condition reads as part of the owner's baseline; that a study which maps file ids to spaces after a run, to attribute gate errors, needs the old and new id of every file the ingest makes; and, from the Compose federation (#29, its fact F7), that deleting a directory on 25.0 is asynchronous and that deleting and recreating a name at once can lose files; the ingest extends the wait to every file it removes, as a precaution, until a live check shows a file needs none. Its code review found that a restore which stopped at its first failure, after a start that checked every staged source first, kept every later file missing through every later run; that a failed arrival or restore was scored unless the executor remembered to ask; and that an id made by an upload or a restore that then failed was never recorded. The first study's record checker found (#262) that a snapshot was matched to its plan by the arriving files' labels alone: a plan whose every part was 300 s later passed, so an analysis reading arrivals "relative to the plan" could pair a run with the wrong plan unnoticed. *Test:* files due at 100 s and 300 s are absent before and present after, each write recorded after its due time and in order; a file in four parts is absent, then a quarter, a half, three quarters and whole; an arrival waits for a second provider that shows new files after 6 s; a provider that never shows the file and a write answered 500 are `ingest_failure`, with the later part abandoned and the kind refused as a treatment; a differing file the ingest did not make and a staged source changed after load refuse the start with no delete sent, and so does a file with an absent file's bytes whose staged copy is damaged, which stays; a note the ingest wrote whose staged copy is damaged during the run stays too, its restore fails alone (a day listed after it is still written back) and the post-run check refuses the attempt as `ingest_failure`, as does a complete file whose copy is damaged between the start's restore and its removal, which stays, with no delete sent; a part still being written past the stop's bound fails the restore rather than racing it; after the run the complete file is back whole and the absent one gone, with another file and the staged copies untouched; a run that ends before an arrival restores from the staged copy and is no failure; a crash mid-plan and a crash right after the start's removal both end at the baseline after the next start, the second with its old id read from the journal; the id events and the journal match the fake's ids; the snapshot holds no provider name, path or staging directory; an empty plan records the same steps; the plan refuses each malformed key and its fingerprint follows the schedule and every staged byte; an upload's stop leaves a verified prefix that a later upload resumes into the same file, and an upload without a stop of a local file that grows meanwhile fails; one staged copy changed during a run, or before a start, leaves the file after it restored, and the failure names only the first; an id made by a restore whose confirmation fails, by a restore's upload that fails after creating the file and by an arrival whose write fails is in the snapshot and the journal; the post-run check passes a clean run and refuses a failed arrival, a failed restore and a run it cannot vouch for, naming the step and nothing private; through the scheduler, a failed arrival with no `raise_if_failed` is charged infrastructure of `ingest_failure` and run again; a change log another user wrote, before the ingest's claim or after it, is refused, and a teardown over the ingest's log is refused through either provisioner; a snapshot binds to the plan that made it, loaded again, and a plan of the same labels with the day 300 s later, another staged source or size, another prefix, part count, spacing or baseline, or the other mode is refused naming the file and the field (the real snapshots of an arrival and of a preallocated run, read back as JSON, included), a plan that differs only in a file's path is refused by the hash, a snapshot without its plan, its hash, a file's field or its steps is refused as unverifiable, a reading at another due time, of an unknown kind or with an unhashable label is refused though the summary agrees, a start that says neither that it set the start state nor that it did not earns no exemption from the planned readings, and a snapshot from before the mode existed binds to its arrival plan alone.

**O28. A provider is taken down only on sanction and only to be measured, is restored in `finally` and shown restored, and what the outage answered is recorded as it came.** The day-0 outage check stops a provider's service only through the fault injector of #17 (O10, O11), with all of `EXPCORE_LIVE_MUTATE=1`, `EXPCORE_LIVE_FAULTS=1` and `sanctioned = true` in `[faults]` of platform.toml, and with the federation's lock held exclusively for the space's whole scenario; without any one it is unknown and sends nothing. It stops the service only after the scenario is proved by the local provider's own block map (a file held by the data provider alone, a file replicated at a mirror, nothing at the local provider, three different providers) and a baseline came back whole with everything up: the REST reads, the reads through the mount of a real sandbox in mount mode, and the agent's knowledge tools called through MCP; a scenario that cannot be proved, or a baseline that does not serve, stops nothing and is unknown. A provider counts as down only when the injector sees its service down and it answers no REST call, and as restored only when the injector clears the injection with the service running under an identity other than the one journalled before the stop and the provider serving (#27's readiness wait), the provider answers again and reads back what was written; readiness alone is not a restore, and a stop whose outcome is unknown is waited out before the start. A restore the injector cannot clear or verify, a provider that does not answer REST again and a read that does not return what was written fail the check, and halt the spaces after it. The restore runs in a `finally`, whatever a measurement raised, and before the scratch is torn down (teardown deletes through the provider that was down); the scratch's absence is verified. Each cell (a read through REST or the mount, a listing, a tool call) records the seconds it took against its bound and an outcome from one vocabulary: `served`, `partial`, `differs`, `refused`, `no answer`, `blocked`; a REST answer its status and Onedata's error id, a mount read its errno by name and number, its exception class and whether it blocked, a tool call whether it timed out, its attempts and its wording as a digest with its error terms. A call that waited as long as it was allowed is `blocked`, never `refused`; a listing cut partway is `partial` with what it got; what a call answered is a fact and not a failure; a mount the platform declares that was not read, a knowledge tool not called and anything else not measured make the check unknown. Providers are `providers[i]`, spaces `spaces[i]`, files the pair and kind they are, a service's identity a SHA-256 digest; only structured fields are kept, and a tool's answer or wording, which can hold names the check does not know (a user's id, a provider's registered name, an address), only as a SHA-256 prefix of it with every known name and id-like token replaced, its length and its error terms. *Learned:* the first federation study could not design an outage condition without knowing what reads and the knowledge plane do while a provider is down (#108); the first round of the check copied the injector's stop and start without its journal or lock, skipped the mount and read the knowledge plane as admin REST calls, which is not what the agent sees. The post-merge review of the check (#178) found two holes in it: it prepared the scenario's files before it built the injector, so a process that died with the data provider stopped made the preparation fail against the stopped provider and return before the journal's recovery ran, leaving the provider down with no restore recorded; and it read both mount files after the restore but judged only the owner-only one. *Test:* the outage goes through the injector (journalled before the stop, settled after, the federation held exclusively by an outage run naming the target), and a stop of unknown outcome is waited out and restored; a stopped service that is not seen down, and one seen down whose REST still answers, are no outage; every consent, the sanction and a scenario that is not as built each stop nothing; a start that keeps the identity, a service that does not run again, a failed stop command and a provider that does not answer after the restore each fail the check, and a measurement that raises still restores; the mount is read in the agent's container of a sandbox on the sandbox's fake host, a blocked read and an ETIMEDOUT each recorded as they came, and the mount cell itself, run on real files, reads, names an errno and kills a read that blocks; a declared mount without a host to start the sandbox on, or with a sandbox that does not start, is unknown; the four tools are called through a stand-in MCP server, with the replication's target the local provider and the providers asked of the space under test (a wrong id fails), `get_file_distribution` also of each file read during the outage, and a tool whose schema cannot be filled leaves the check unknown, a tool's error wording is kept as its digest and terms with its attempts, and a user's id, a foreign provider's name and an address in a tool's answers reach no fact, a tool that never answers is blocked at the bound, and tools that fail with everything up stop nothing; no provider, space, service, host or id is in the report; with several spaces each is run in turn, the live helpers read every space's facts, and a provider not shown back stops the rest; a journal showing the data provider stopped by an earlier run is recovered before the first space prepares anything, with the provider answering again when the preparation begins, the recovery recorded in the space's facts and the run then going on as usual, and a recovery that cannot be done prepares nothing and halts the spaces after it; after the restore the replicated file read through the mount, as the owner-only one, must return what was written, and one that differs or fails with an errno fails the check, while one wrong answer followed by the right bytes passes.

**O29. A plumbing read that times out is tried again, boundedly and in the log; a measurement and a write never are.** The client tries a GET again only when it timed out (an `httpx` read, connect or pool timeout): three attempts in all, pauses of 2 s and 5 s, each retry on a connection of its own, never one of the pool's, and the connection that stalled closed and dropped; within the call's own deadline when it has one. Never retried: a PUT, POST (reading POSTs included), PATCH or DELETE, an error answer of any status, a connection error (but under a reset reading's policy, below), a write timeout, and a streamed or range read; `get` and the reads that find a target's space take `retry`, and `mutate`, `read_range` and `read_content` take `gate_retry`, which applies to their gate's reads alone, so nothing that writes or streams can be handed a policy for itself. **The mutation gate is plumbing:** every mutation, range read and streamed range first reads the space of its target (the first chunk of a new file's upload reads the new file's parent from the holder, an answer nothing has cached), and those reads are retried under the caller's policy; the gate stays closed, since each attempt that is answered decides from its own answer, nothing earlier is reused, and after the third timeout the target is unread and nothing is sent (`ScopeUnread`). **The polls that end an operation retry** as the reset's own cancel wait does: `replicate`, `evict` and the bulk eviction, and so the reset's eviction stage, poll a transfer's record under the provisioner's policy, and `place_ranges` reads what the receiver holds before and after its placement, outside any poll, under it too. Every read names its policy at its call site (`retry=`, or `gate_retry=`): the provisioner's listings, size and attribute reads, visibility wait, gate reads and those polls, `read_file`'s size read, the reset's reads, the ingest's attribute reads and visibility waits, and the root and the gate a day-0 mutating check writes under retry; the health gate's reads, the placement probes' reads and block maps, the transfer log, day-0's reading checks, the pre-campaign content check and the reads inside a poll that already counts a failed read as one that did not match keep one attempt, so a stall stays the recorded stall it is. **A reset's readings and each stage's transfer reads also retry a connection failure** (O46, O51): each reading's or stage's policy (`Provisioner.reading_retry`, four attempts, 2, 5 and 15 s apart) tries again a GET whose connection failed, was reset or was cut before its answer, never an answer, and never a failure after an error status had arrived; an exhausted read is unread and fails closed; it is made only by the reset's readings (`READING_MADE`), never by a measuring module. Each timed-out attempt and the outcome of the attempt after a retry are logged (the seconds, the attempt, the connection as new, reused or unknown, and for a reused one how many requests it had served; a provider by position, no path, id or token), and each retry is counted: the reset's record and the ingest's snapshot say `reads_retried`. *Learned:* #185, from the first federation study's ingest, whose attribute read on a provider hung past its timeout twice and then answered in 0.01 s on a fresh connection, and whose pilot runs showed every stalled read on a reused keep-alive connection and the retry on a new one answering in about 0.02 s; without a bounded retry the same hang ends an upload or a reset, and with an unbounded or silent one a probe or a health check would hide the stall it exists to see. *Test:* a read that times out once and then answers succeeds with one retry, 2 s later, logged and counted; three timeouts fail as a transport failure saying three attempts were made, with all three logged and both pauses taken; a read, connect and pool timeout are each retried, and a write timeout, a connection error, an error answer of every status, a PUT, POST, PATCH, DELETE, a reading POST, a streamed read and a range read are one request each, whatever policy their gate was given; only reads take `retry` and only `mutate`, `read_range` and `read_content` take `gate_retry`; a gate read that hangs once is asked again and the mutation is sent once, one that hangs three times refuses it unsent, the answer of the attempt that was answered decides (a space renamed meanwhile is refused), a gate with no policy is one attempt, the first chunk of a new file's upload is written once after its parent read was asked again, a verification, a range read and a streamed read are retried at their gate and not past the stream's deadline, each read of a streamed range's gate being given what remains of the deadline when it begins (three gate reads that each stall twice before they answer end within twice the deadline, not near three times, and the last read's retry is cut to the 4 s left), and a transfer poll of `replicate`, `evict`, the bulk eviction and the reset's eviction stage that hangs once is asked again within the wait's bound while a block-map read is one attempt unless its caller, `place_ranges` before and after its placement, names a policy;
