against a fake data provider with an imported storage and its panel, the storage's flags checked and each mismatch refused, the scan waited for and its failures and its bound, a file missing, of another size or beyond the manifest failing the import with no path in the message, a file not whole on the data provider, a file whose bytes differ, a mirror's listing waited for, a forced re-scan keeping every id and one that re-imports a deleted file under a new id, the force-start gated on the space's live name, the panel's 403 raised as an answer; an uploaded space checked by the same code; the round trip unknown in such a space; and gated live tests comparing an imported and an uploaded copy of one tree for the tree, the bytes, the ids, a re-scan, the owner's block maps, an on-demand read, a replication, the reset, a QoS requirement and the attributes.

**O42. A teardown ends within its deadline, whatever the host does, and says how it ended.** `SandboxPlatform` waited for the sandbox's stop for ever, and the commands under it (`docker rm` and the others) were waited for after their kill with no bound, so a teardown wedged on a FUSE mount whose provider is gone held the run, the worker and the process. The platform now waits for the stop at most `teardown_deadline_s` (600 s by default) and records `completed`, `failed` or `cut` under `teardown` in the sandbox's metadata, with the deadline, the seconds and, for a cut, what the stop had left (a phase and counts, never a name). A cut is `InfraError("sandbox")`, so the run is infrastructure and never the agent's doing, unless the body's own error stands; the teardown goes on in a daemon thread, the lease's probe keeps the key quarantined until the sandbox is shown gone, and a cancellation pending at the cut is kept. A command the host runs that does not end after its kill is given up on after `KILL_WAIT_S`, and is the timeout, with the output it had written. *Learned:* the first real study's Day-0 wiring, a sidecar wedged on a FUSE mount whose provider was gone (#200, #190). *Test:* a wedged removal is cut at a short deadline with the left-over recorded, a completed and a failed teardown are recorded as such, a body's error and a cancellation are kept, a cut keeps the key quarantined; a child that does not die after its kill is not waited for.

**O43. What is kept of a cell's stdout and stderr is capped, and the cut is recorded.** A 1 MiB read printed about 1.4 MB, all of it read into the harness's memory and kept, so a cell's output was bounded by nothing. The sandbox keeps the first 1 MiB of each stream (a whole number of characters), reads and drops the rest in a thread of its own so the cell never blocks on a full pipe, and records on the cell, and on its result, how many bytes each cut stream wrote and how many were kept (`output_cut`). The code tool ends the text it returns with a notice that says the same and flags the call's `source_truncated`; the loop's cap (`output_cap_chars`) then applies to that whole text, so the sandbox cuts first, the tool adds its notice, and the loop cuts last, and a cap below the text cuts the notice off with the tail and leaves the loop's own notice instead (a cap too short for that notice cuts plain, and one a few characters below the text leaves a piece of the sandbox's), the same on every run. *Learned:* the first real study's Day-0 wiring (#200). *Test:* a command that writes 100 kB keeps the cap and counts the rest, 24 MB are written with the allocation of the caller flat, a cut never splits a character, a large input does not deadlock a command that writes as it reads; a cell's cut is on its result and its record, and the model's text says so; for a cell that prints 3 MiB, the text the model receives is fixed exactly under a cap of 10,000 characters (the loop's notice alone), under 2 MiB (the sandbox's notice last) and at the two caps between.

**O44. A file that is still arriving shows its final size from the start and holds only what was written, its baseline comes back whole without being removed, and no write of content truncates.** In a progressive ingest's preallocated mode each planned file belongs to the dataset's baseline. Before the start's restore or its first removal, every planned file's staged copy must be there with the plan's size and SHA-256, and before the first removal each file's holdings must be what the plan declares: every provider its `replicas` name holding it whole by that provider's own block map, and no other provider but the data provider a byte of it; otherwise the start is refused with nothing removed. At the start the complete file is removed (its staged copy checked, the intent journalled, its old id answered absent on every provider before the name is used again), made again under its name and extended first, by an empty write at its final size, and only then given its declared prefix at offset 0, so the namespace shows the final size from the first moment the file holds a byte. The start state is confirmed before the schedule begins: the prefix's hash read back, every provider that must see the file showing its final size, and every such provider's block map showing the data provider holding exactly the prefix and no provider a byte beyond it. The schedule is anchored at the agent's first model turn, through the first-turn hook, and a run whose first turn never came is refused. Before each part the data provider's block map must be exactly the ranges written so far, or the part fails closed with nothing written; after it the hash read back and the block maps must show exactly the written ranges at the final size; any failure is `ingest_failure`. The reset writes what the block map lacks from the staged source at its offsets, writes the file whole again if its hash still differs, and verifies it, never removing it; a file the ingest did not make is kept only when its bytes are its staged source's, but an empty one the journal says the start was making (a creation whose answer was lost) is removed and the file written back whole. The reset then replicates the completed file to each provider its `replicas` name, verified by that provider's block map, so a run that reads through the mirror next starts from the baseline's mirror state. The mirror runs that replication from its own dbsync copy of the owner's file location, which lags the owner's write while the block map read through the mirror already shows the owner whole, so a replication scheduled right after the reset's write can copy only the prefix and report `completed`: while the mirror's own block map is short after a replication ended, it is scheduled again after a pause, bounded by the settle bound (O01), and each try is recorded. The snapshot maps each id a file had to the one the ingest gave it in its place (`id_map`) and times the start (`start_s`). The journal carries baseline marks, each on disk before the step it announces: `open` before the start changes anything, `unhealthy` when a start, a reset or a recovery fails after that, `whole` once a restore verifies every file. A start is held back, with nothing sent, unless the last mark is `whole`, as a hold that costs no attempt (O45); the start's own recovery, when it verifies every file, marks it `whole`; otherwise `ProgressiveIngest.recover` is the way back. The health check, given the ingests' journals, fails while a baseline is `unhealthy` or `open` with no live attempt owning it (O45), so a sparse or missing baseline never stays in place unflagged. A write of content without an offset, which truncates the file to 0 first, is refused by the client before anything is read or sent; an upload over a longer target is refused rather than truncated, and a longer link-check probe gives way to the next generation. *Learned:* #107, ruled on 10 and 11 Nov for a study whose "still arriving" state must look, in the namespace, as the baseline does: the reset as the arrival mode has it would remove 14 days of a dataset every run, and REST has no truncate to a size, so a file the owner holds whole can only be made again; the Compose federation measured an unwritten range reading as zeros through REST and the mount, the block map holding only written ranges and the empty write extending the size with no block (`deploy/compose-federation/day0/2026-11-10-federation-facts.md`, `2026-11-11-federation-facts-mount.md`), and op-worker f118ed9's source shows the empty write's zero-size block dropped and a write without `offset` truncating to 0 (file_content_rest_handler.erl:224-228), which the coordinator ruled must be refused in code, not only never sent. On 14 Nov the coordinator made four conditions of the merge: every staged copy verified before any removal; the reset restoring each file whole and the mirror's replica of each file the mirror's baseline holds (an outage run after a preallocated one must start from the right mirror state); the run's old-to-new ids recorded; and any failure mid-start or mid-reset marking the federation unhealthy, as the fault injector's open injection does, and refusing later starts. The live slot of 14 Nov ran the gated mirror test: the reset's replication to the mirror reported `completed` with the mirror holding the prefix alone, 1,577,178 B short, nothing scheduled it again, and the restore failed after the 300 s settle bound; op-worker f118ed9's synchronizer reads the target's dbsync copy of the other providers' file locations, which no REST call shows (`deploy/compose-federation/day0/2026-11-14-federation-facts-replication-lag.md`). *Test:* the client refuses a content write with no offset, an empty or other query, a negative, boolean, fractional or string offset and a trailing slash, sending nothing, and sends an offset write and an empty one; the fake extends a file by an empty write with no block, reads an unwritten range as zeros through any provider and places no block for it, and truncates on a write without an offset; a preallocated file has its final size before any byte is written and no block anywhere, a range is written at its offsets and holds exactly its blocks, a range into a file another made or outside the source is refused, and a prefix is read back by its hash without reading past it; a plan's mode and prefix are checked at load, an arrival plan keeps its hash, and the fingerprint follows the mode and the prefix; after the start the namespace shows the final size on both providers with only the prefix held, the new file extended before its prefix; the parts fall due from the first turn and the block map grows by exactly each; the core's first-turn hook anchors it and an arrival ingest has none; a part with the wrong hash fails closed and the reset rewrites the file whole; a block beyond the written ranges, held by a provider of the space or by one platform.toml does not declare, fails the next part with nothing written; a creation whose answer was lost is taken back and the file written whole, and one whose removal failed is removed by the recovery, the next start refused until then, but stays, with nothing deleted, while its staged copy is damaged; a change-log line a crash cut short is no line, the next starts clean, and any other unreadable line fails closed; a part out of order is refused; a run without its first turn is refused, its snapshot not ok, and still restored; a file neither the ingest's nor its staged source refuses the start with nothing sent; the reset completes the file in place with no DELETE and its id kept; a crash mid-run, a crash between the removal and the new file and one before the extension each end with the complete file after the recovery, every start before it refused; a damaged or missing staged copy of a later file refuses the start with nothing created, written or deleted; the reset replicates the recreated file to its declared mirror, verified by the mirror's block map, fails when the replication places nothing, and fails when the owner's block map stays short though the hash matches; a replication the mirror runs from a lagging copy of the owner's map copies the prefix alone and reports completed, and is scheduled again, so the mirror ends whole and the restore records both tries and their ids; a copy that never catches up fails the reset closed within the settle bound, the tries named and the baseline marked unhealthy; a start with a lagging mirror schedules no replication and keeps its record; the fake's replication copies only what its target's lagging view showed, while the block map through any provider shows the source's current blocks; a replica the plan does not declare refuses the start with nothing removed, and one on the data provider is refused when the instrument is built; the snapshot maps each recreated file's old id to its new one and records the start's seconds; a process that dies after the removal, the creation, the extension or the prefix, or mid-reset, leaves its baseline open with a holder that is gone, so the health check fails and every start is refused with nothing sent until the recovery brings the file and its mirror's replica back whole; an in-process failure at each of those steps whose recovery fails too marks the baseline unhealthy with its phase, file and step, with the same refusals, and a refused start's finish touches nothing; a failed start whose own recovery verifies every file leaves the baseline whole for the next start; a recovery is refused while another live process holds the baseline open; a journal line a crash cut short is cut off before the next line is written, so no mark is glued to it; a start admitted between another's mark check and its mark refuses the other, which never touches its files; a recovery with a plan that leaves out the run's mirror or file is refused with nothing sent; a new journal and its directories are pushed to disk with the first mark, and an instrument's first line syncs the journal's whole path even when a killed writer left the journal there; the marks' reader skips a torn last line, fails closed on any other unreadable line or unknown state, and the platform's health check fails on an unhealthy baseline before it asks the federation anything; an empty preallocated plan passes; an upload over a longer target is refused and a changed one rewritten at explicit offsets; a probe with other bytes is rewritten at offset 0 and a longer one replaced by the next generation; a gated live test checks the extension, the gap's zeros and the instrument end to end.

**O45. A preallocated ingest's mark names its attempt: a start it holds back costs no attempt, and a mark no live attempt owns is unhealthy at once.** Each baseline mark records its owner, `expcore.campaign.liveness.owner()`: the attempt the start ran in (its campaign and the store's id of its row) and the process by boot id, pid and start time; the start also takes the baseline's hold, an exclusive `flock` beside the journal, before it marks the baseline open, and lets it go after the restore's `whole` mark, as its attempt ends, when its instrument is collected, or with its process. An `open` mark is held while its hold is taken and its owner is not shown ended; it is orphaned, as unhealthy as an `unhealthy` mark, the moment its attempt has ended (finished or set aside, by the store), its campaign is closed, its process is gone, or its hold was let go (an attempt of another process that ended, an instrument dropped) or never taken, with no wait for the run's ceiling. A start on a held, orphaned or `unhealthy` baseline is held back with nothing sent as `ingest_held`, registered untreatable and uncharged (C33), and the post-run check after it refuses with the same kind; a start that changed the baseline and failed stays `ingest_failure`. The health check given the journals fails, charging no run, on an orphaned or unhealthy baseline, and defers, charging none, on a held one in a check outside any attempt, so the campaign holds its runs until the run's reset; inside an attempt a held mark reads healthy, except to the attempt that owns it, whose ingest then ended without its reset; a failure wins over a deferral, and charges only the attempt that wrote the failing mark, so a bystander's post-run check sets it aside uncharged. A recovery is refused on a held baseline unless this process took it outside any attempt, and on one whose hold cannot be read, which is unknown, never free; it lets go of a hold this process keeps. *Learned:* the follow-up to #246 (14 Nov): an open mark held by a live process (a run in progress, or an instrument dropped without `restore()` in the campaign's own process) read healthy, so the gate stayed open while every start was refused as `ingest_failure`, charged, and refused attempts piled up until the runs were exhausted. *Test:* a mark names its attempt inside one and its process outside; an open mark whose attempt has ended, in any state, reads unhealthy at once without charging, and so after its campaign closed; one whose process died reads unhealthy, with or without an attempt; one whose hold another live process let go reads unhealthy at once; a hold is let go as its attempt ends and when its holder is collected, and an instrument dropped outside any attempt leaves its baseline orphaned once collected, until a recovery; one a live attempt owns holds its starts, refuses a recovery, defers the checks outside an attempt, passes another attempt's post-run check and fails, charging, its own; an unhealthy mark charges only the attempt that wrote it; a failed space wins over a held one, and the judged attempt's own failure over another's; a hold that cannot be read refuses a recovery and holds starts back; a held start raises `ingest_held`, uncharged, sends nothing and its check refuses the same way, and a fault injector whose restore then fails keeps it, noting the failure; through the scheduler a start held three times with `max_attempts` 2 runs once the holder's reset marks the baseline whole, uncharged; with the journals the campaign begins no attempt while the baseline is held and asks again no sooner than `recheck_s`; an attempt that drops its instrument fails its own post-run check, its mark orphaned the moment its result is stored, the gate closing without a charge until `recover()`, after which the run is done.
