and no verb acts on the agent's node, or on anything but a provider's or the zone's service, and the relay on nothing but a provider's. **The relay and every Python the HPC driver launches run isolated and write no bytecode** (`-I -B`, the core's own five launches on a node too: the sandbox's gate, the mount watcher, the egress collector, the completion check and the copy-out, by a scan of every `-I` display of `src/`): the relay's `serve`, `alive` and `stop`, the verbs' argv prefix (`<python> -I -B hpcfed.py`, also the `[faults] command` `up` renders), the shard step and the shards' merge (run by `collect --run` in the 0700 directory it collected into) never import a module from the directory they were launched from, nor read a `PYTHON*` variable, and the relay works in the federation's root, a 0700 directory of ours that no symbolic link reaches (refused otherwise, and by `up`'s preflight before anything is submitted), whatever directory `up` was launched from, so the verbs it runs inherit that root; its environment is PATH, HOME and LC_ALL (and SLURM_CONF) alone. *Learned:* the HPC federation's process model (deploy/hpc-federation `DESIGN.md`, M1) and the coordinator's decision for a login-node relay, 16 Nov 2026; the first live `down` with a relay running (16 Nov), which failed on a login node whose kernel has no `pidfd_open`: the stop's raw call answered ENOSYS, so `down` could not verify a relay it then ended itself, and every `down` failed until a second one; and F2, seen live on 16 Nov 2026: a `down` launched from the login node's `/tmp`, which held 49 `.py` files of other users, had the relay's `stop` (then `python -B -m`) import another user's `/tmp/inspect.py` in place of the standard library's, and list the relay unverified. The fallback and the second check are tested offline; the live `down` with them is not yet run, and nothing else of it has run live. And the first live relayed verbs (17 Nov), each of which took about 30 s (median 28.7 s) though the relay saw the request in 0.04-0.14 s and ran the verb in a median of 0.9 s: the client polled the answer's path, and the node's first lookup, made before the answer existed, cached its absence until the directory's attributes expired. The fix and its tests model the node's negative lookup cache over the real file system; no live federation has yet re-measured the latency. And a diagnosis from live evidence (17 Nov 2026) of the relay's timed-out verbs: at the bound `subprocess.run` ended only the `hpcfed.py` process, its `srun` child was orphaned and kept waiting on the controller (silent for up to its message timeout, about 200 s), and when it caught up the step was made and launched after the relay had answered `timed_out`, with hpcfed's own cancel path never run. The group kill and the watch are tested over real processes (a stand-in verb whose child outlives it, a real relay process) and a stand-in Slurm in which a step named for a timed-out verb appears late and is cancelled by its exact id, a step of another name, tag, word or job is never touched, a look whose job check or second listing fails still cancels the step it found, and a window the controller never answered in is never recorded as nothing seen; a fresh-context review found that hpcfed's own cancel path can end an srun after its own bound while the controller still holds the step, a verb that exits inside the relay's bound with no watch, which a verb that ran 25 s or more is now watched for; no live federation has run them. The first live provider restart after a fault-injected stop (17 Nov 2026) failed its identity check on a service that had not changed: the check ran 1.9 s before op_worker's Onezone connection came up, and compared the word null (`jsonget`'s print of a JSON null) with the recorded domain; a second start, where the connection came first, passed. Tested offline over a `/configuration` that answers null for its first reads; the live restart with the wait is not yet run. And the first three provider outages on one HPC federation (17 Nov), which measured the stop and the relay: a service ended 26.1 s, 27.1 s and 33.1 s after its SIGTERM, against the layout's 600 s `stop_seconds`, and relay verb steps started 47 to 103 s after they were issued during a heal, one round trip taking 104.6 s against the relay's 120 s command bound. Every committed layout and the `[hpc]` default now take `stop_seconds` 50 (the largest stop plus 50 %) and a declared `step_launch_wait_s` 240, rendered into `[faults]`: a control step's bound is waited for from its client's start, so it must hold the step's launch wait besides its work, and a step's creation can wait on the scheduler's controller up to its message timeout (measured 47 to 103 s, up to 200 s possible on the site, plus 20 %); 103 s plus a 33 s stop was past the stop's control step at `stop_seconds` + 60 s. The launch wait is added to every bound that waits on a step's client from its start (the stop, status, exec and mount-check steps, `down`'s teardown step, a start's wait to see its service step listed, and every relay verb's bound). Each relay verb has a bound of its own, derived from its inner worst case and 30 s and never below the declared floor (`command_timeout_s`, the launch wait and 60 s, 300 s): a status 600 s, a start 630 s, a ping and a link restart 570 s, a stop 450 s (`settle_timeout_s`'s default); one bound of 300 s was below a start after an unrecorded stop (600 s) and a restarted shared-import owner's status (570 s), so the relay would have timed out a verb about to complete, and a restore after a stop of unknown outcome would have failed when it matters. The least `[tokens] margin_s` is 5,370 s. The launch wait widens timeouts only, how long the harness waits before it fails closed: it never lengthens an outage or a hold. The launch wait, the floor and every verb's bound are in the faults fingerprint after their defaults, a verb past its bound is still answered `timed_out` and read as a command with no answer, never as done, and the platform's declared deferral counts the launch wait through the heal's reserves.

**O59. A tree several federations import is read-only for every one of them, and nothing a federation does writes to it.** A space on a directory of the cluster's shared filesystem that other federations import too is declared `shared` (`import = { readonly = true, shared = true, ... }`). The layout refuses `shared` unless the import is read-only and names its manifest, two spaces on one tree, a manifest inside a tree and a direct mount of a writable import; the platform file refuses `shared` beside `readonly = false` at load, enters it in the fingerprint when true, and the check of the owner's panel refuses a storage whose `readonly` is not the boolean `true`, before the scan is read; the check after up refuses a platform file that leaves the sharing out, and the HPC driver runs it over the rendered file before the link check. The driver's preflight refuses, before anything is submitted, an import that is not shared, a variable that does not name a usable tree or manifest, a manifest larger than its space's support, two trees that overlap, a root or log directory that is, holds or lies inside a tree (by real path), and the core's step, whose allowlist carries none of the variables. The owner's service binds the tree read-only, from a list a node-side script checks (a plain path, no link on the way, not on node-local memory, outside the work directory) at configuration and again at every start, and a control step on each owner's node, run by `up` before the bootstrap starts a scan, proves the mount read-only from the mounts the running service holds. A reset of such a space evicts the mirrors and removes the run's own requirements, writes no file, and is refused when its plan names the owner. *Learned:* what a federation on a shared tree must never rely on is a flag it set itself: the kernel's mount options are the proof, the panel's flag and the core's refusal are the layers around it; the storage a support names was the first of the provider's, which is arbitrary once a provider has an imported storage beside its own (the Compose bootstrap names it, the first HPC run's `onboard.py` still takes the first, so it is not used for a provider that owns an import). *Test:* the declaration's forms, refusals and fingerprint in the platform file and in the layout; the manifest a variable names and where it may lie; the owner's panel showing a writable storage, or a flag that is not the boolean `true`, refused before the scan; the verification passing, and failing for a missing file and one of another size, naming no path; the check after up over the rendered platform file, and its refusals before any call; every request of a reset on such a space, none of which writes under `/data/` or evicts from the owner; the node-side script on a tree of each kind it refuses (relative, `..`, link, missing, not a directory, unreadable, on node-local memory, inside the work directory), its mountpoints and its bind arguments, the list checked again at the start, and the mount check on mount tables that are read-only, writable, absent and mixed; the HPC driver's `up` over the fake Slurm (each owner's `--import` after the peers and a mirror's none, no tree path in any step's environment or in a control step, the mount check on each owner's node with the node's Python after every verdict and before the core, a failed proof, and a report that exits 0 but proves nothing (empty, unparsable, a space missing, doubled, unasked, unheld or writable), failing `up` naming the provider and space and no path, each refusal before any command, the core's phase refusing to start over a partial up, every call at its place, a down that is the down of any federation, and every tree unchanged); the bootstrap's order (the import storage before the support, the scan awaited last), its storage ids, its LUMA mappings as `luma_requests` gives them on the storage a support names, the core's phase verifying before the link check and refusing a platform file that leaves the sharing out, the direct mount's table; the `pilot-shape-import` layout and template; and gated live tests of the same checks on a live federation, with the tree described before and after.

**O60. A mount run on an HPC node, where the agent shares oneclient's uid, is guarded and reported, and a missing or damaged guard log or an unproven isolation is never read as clean.** On a batch node the agent's cells (setuid Apptainer) and oneclient run as one uid, with no root, no user namespace and FUSE without `user_allow_other`, so a mount is visible only to the uid that made it, and "the agent holds no token" cannot be enforced as the Docker sidecar enforces it: a process of that uid that can name oneclient's pid can read its environment, which holds the token, and trace it. The study's PI ruled (16 Nov 2026) that the mount path stays on HPC with detection and report, as for egress (O52). The Apptainer backend's mount helper (`apptainer/hostmount.py`, hook c) writes the token to a 0600 file in a 0700 directory of the run's that no cell binds, starts oneclient on the node through a wrapper that reads the file into oneclient's own environment (read-only, without `allow_other`, with the core's options), removes the file once the mount is up, and binds only the mount point into each cell. A watcher outside the cells (`sandbox/mountwatch.py`, `sandbox/mountproof.py`), started once the mount is up, holds inotify watches on the token file and its directory, opens the agent's window when both watches see the host remove the token (the canary), samples oneclient's `TracerPid`, and proves from the node's `/proc` that each cell of the run it finds (a process of this uid in a nested PID namespace whose mount table holds the run's FUSE device) is in a PID namespace of its own while oneclient is in the node's, with no `proc` mount of the host's and no protected directory in its mounts. A process of the node's PID namespace that holds the run's mount anywhere but the host's mount point is a cell without a namespace of its own, and a cell still alive when the window closes is a failed proof. The core reads the log (`expcore.mountguard`): it is intact only when present, parseable, numbered without a gap, of this run's nonce and closed, with both watches set, the canary, oneclient seen, one window, no overflow and no unreadable sample; the isolation is proven only with the run's mount seen, every poll whole, at least one proof and none failed, and never from a cut log. A run is `flagged` for an event in the window (`token_access`) or a tracer (`process_access`), `unknown` for an unknown log or an unproven isolation, and otherwise `clean`; every run that is not clean is listed in the report's `## Mount isolation (detected, not prevented)` and left out of a rerun of the preregistered analysis, as for egress; a batch checked by both guards also gets the rerun without every run either leaves out (flagged or unknown to either), with the runs left out per condition and by which guard. The mount's token file is listed in the run's record before it is written (the hook's `token_paths()`), so the reaper of a driver killed outright removes it before it unmounts anything. *Learned:* the PI's ruling of 16 Nov; the HPC probe of #179 (no user namespaces, `/dev/fuse` 0666 with a setuid `fusermount3`, no `user_allow_other`, `/proc` mounted `hidepid=invisible`); and this change's pipeline test, in which the record's redaction took the access counts' key `token` for a secret's and replaced it, so the counts are `token_file` and `token_dir`. **Limits:** inotify names no process, so every event in the window counts as the agent's; it sees an open or a read, never a read of memory the file was mapped into before the window; a tracer is sampled, so one that attaches and leaves between samples is missed; oneclient's environment and memory are covered only by the proof that no cell can see its process, never watched; a cell that starts and ends between two polls is not proved itself (a run needs one proved cell, and every cell has the same command line); a process of the uid that is not a cell (an ssh login into the node, a job step) is outside every check, hence the operational rule the study guide states: no interactive login and no other job step of the account may run on a sandbox node while runs are live. **Not run live:** whether setuid Apptainer binds into a cell a FUSE mount point made without `allow_other`, and a oneclient that runs natively on a node. *Test:* the reader keeps intact only a whole log with its canary and its window and names each damage, and the driver's own read of oneclient's log in the window (`mount_log()`) is no access, the watcher watching the token file and its directory alone; and a cut log never proves the isolation; the verdict is flagged for an access or a tracer whatever else holds, and unknown for a missing, failed or contradictory snapshot or an unproven isolation; the proof passes a nested cell with the run's mount and fails one with the host's `/proc`, one binding a directory that holds or lies under a protected one, oneclient outside the node's namespace, a cell in the node's namespace and a cell alive at the close, and a failed listing or an unreadable nested process is a failed poll; the watcher, run as its own process on real inotify, is armed by the token's removal and counts a listing of the token's directory, and the core reads its log; the helper writes the token 0600 and removes it before any cell, passes it in no argument, runs oneclient read-only without `allow_other`, refuses a direct mount, a port among the extra arguments and an unpinned binary, and stops idempotently, failing when the mount stays; through the sandbox every cell binds the mount point alone, and the collection closes the window and copies the log; the report, `analysis.json` and the terminal carry the verdicts and the rerun, and a batch without the instrument is unchanged; the record fixture `08-mount-guard.json` validates, replays and reads clean; the combined rerun leaves out a run flagged by egress alone, by the mount guard alone, by both, and one unknown to one guard and clean to the other, and a batch checked by one guard has none; a dead driver's mount run whose unmount fails, or whose reaper cannot list the processes, still has its token file removed, a record's token path outside the run is never removed, and a helper's token path outside the run is refused before its start.

**O61. On a node whose loopback other users share, the data gate serves only a peer of its own uid, and the cell's connects to port 443 reach the gate and nothing else of the cell's is touched.** **The gate's owner check.** On an HPC batch node there is no network namespace, so the gate listens on the node's loopback, which every other user of the node shares: any of them could connect to it, and the gate adds its data-path token to every read it forwards. With `peer_uid_check` true in its configuration the gate attributes each connection it accepts, on the HTTPS listener and on every `tcp_forwards` one, to the uid of the peer's socket (`gatepeer.py`), from `/proc/net/tcp` and `tcp6`, and serves it only if that is its own uid. It reads the row whose local address and port are the peer's and whose remote address and port are the gate's end, so a row of the gate's own side or of another connection never stands for the peer; IPv4 is mapped into IPv6 on both sides, so a v4-mapped peer of a dual-stack listener matches its IPv4 row; a TIME_WAIT leftover is skipped; a row not found is looked up once more. It refuses, with a reset before any byte is read or written and a `peer_refused` line saying why: another uid, a socket the table shows no owner for (uid 0 or the overflow uid), a peer with no row, no peer address, a fault of the check, and everything when the tables cannot be read. The gate does not start if it runs as one of the two uids the tables also print for an ownerless socket, if it cannot read the table, or if `gatepeer.py` is not beside it. **Off by default:** the key absent or false leaves `GateServer.verify_request` the base server's and loads nothing, so Docker's gate, on a network of its own, is as it was, and a gate directory of the three older files still starts. *Learned:* the bundle's `portshim-owner.h`, which makes the same decision in C for the Onedata services, and the coordinator's instruction (16 Nov) that the Apptainer backend turn it on. **Not run live:** a connection from another uid, which a test cannot make (another uid's rows are written from a real connection's address pair), and a node whose `/proc/net/tcp` lists other users' sockets under `hidepid`. *Test:* a real loopback connection of this process, over IPv4, IPv6 and a v4-mapped peer of a dual-stack listener, is read from the kernel's own tables and served; the same connection is refused by a check for another uid, with the uid the table showed; written tables give another uid, uid 0 and the overflow uid, no row, a row of the gate's side only, a row off by one port or address, a TIME_WAIT row before the live one, a v6 row for a mapped peer, junk lines among the rows and unreadable tables; uid 0 and the overflow uid cannot start a check; through the gate, a peer of the gate's uid is served and one of another is closed unread with one logged line, the accept loop goes on, a failing check refuses, a log that cannot be written (its stderr fallback raising) neither ends the accept loop nor leaves the connection open, a `tcp_forwards` listener is checked alike, a flag that is not a boolean, a gate run as uid 0, unreadable tables and a missing `gatepeer.py` refuse the start, and with the flag off nothing is read and a gate directory of the three older files starts. **The cell's 443 mapper.** The published client dials 443 only, and the gate cannot bind below 1024 on a batch node. The cell loads `gateport.c` (`LD_PRELOAD`, built with the bundle's flags in the cell's image): a TCP connect (protocol TCP: an SCTP or MPTCP stream socket is not) to 127.0.0.1:443 goes to the gate's port, the address kept; any other address or port, every IPv6 destination (`[::1]:443` and `[::ffff:127.0.0.1]:443` included), UDP or a Unix socket goes through unchanged. It reads no byte of an address before its length allows (a short address at the end of the last readable page is the kernel's to refuse), and its one stderr line, written in a constructor before `main()`, is written through a new non-blocking description of a pipe or terminal (`/proc/self/fd/2`) and with SIGPIPE held back, so a closed or full pipe loses the line and never kills or holds the process that loads it, and a regular file is appended to as its program does. It exports `connect` and nothing else, and passes the call on with `dlsym(RTLD_NEXT)`, so it must precede `egresslog.so` in the preload list: the egress log then names the gate's port, and after the egress shim, which goes straight to the kernel, the mapper is never reached. The port is read once at load from the cell's read-only `/etc/expcore/gate.json` (a cleared environment cannot drop it), which decides when it exists, and otherwise from `EXPCORE_GATE_PORT`; a malformed file or variable makes the process redirect nothing and say so once on stderr, never guess a port, and a file that exists is not overridden by the environment. **Why no IPv6:** the core's gate listens on 127.0.0.1 only, so a connect to `[::1]:443` rewritten to `[::1]:<gate port>` would reach whatever other socket holds that address, which on a shared node can be another user's listener, and a cell's request would go to a stranger. The federation's names map to 127.0.0.1, so nothing legitimate dials `::1`; `[::1]:443` and `[::ffff:127.0.0.1]:443` go through as asked. *Learned:* the bundle's `portshim.c` and `egresslog.c`, and the Apptainer backend's contract (the cell's `/etc/expcore/gate.json`, `EXPCORE_GATE_PORT`, the preload before the egress shim). **Not run live:** the shim in a real cell with the published client against the real gate; the tests run it under the host's gcc and Python. *Test:* the shim builds with every warning an error and exports only `connect`; a Python client's connect to 127.0.0.1:443 lands on a listener at the gate's port, every time, and one to `[::1]:443` is not rewritten and reaches no listener at the gate's port on `[::1]` (a stranger's, standing in); the port comes from the file with the environment cleared and the file wins over a different variable; a file with other keys, nested values, escapes and whitespace reads, and each of twenty-four unusable files (a missing or mistyped port or address, a port out of range, with a fraction, an exponent or a leading zero, another address, a key twice, trailing text, bad JSON elsewhere, too deep, too large), a directory and a FIFO in its place, leaves every connection alone with one stderr line that names the file and not the variable; a malformed variable (thirteen forms) does the same with a line that names it, and with no file and no variable the shim is inert and silent; another loopback port, 127.0.0.2:443, UDP, an SCTP socket and an MPTCP socket are untouched, a short socket address is never read past its length, a stderr pipe nobody reads or one that is full neither kills nor holds the process, and against a stand-in for the next `connect()`, other ports, other addresses, a Unix socket, `[::1]:443`, `[::ffff:127.0.0.1]:443` and other IPv6 destinations, and the short 24-byte `[::1]:443` address, reach it unchanged, the rewritten destination and the next connect's own result reach the caller; with the egress shim after it the egress log records the gate's port, and before it the connect stays on 443.

**O62. A shard step killed outright resumes from its last good snapshot, consistent and verified, and the spend the kill took out of its store is counted once.** A #154 shard on an HPC federation runs its dispatcher on the sandbox node with its store on the node's tmpfs (SQLite's locks), so a step killed outright (walltime, a node's end, an OOM in the step's cgroup) lost the whole of its `out/`. **Snapshots** (`expcore.campaign.out_snapshot`, driven by `deploy/hpc-federation/shards.py`): every `snapshot_every_s` (900 s by default, a manifest key) and within 30 s of a run's end (a new attempt marker; the store is never opened for the poll, since a read-only open of a WAL store leaves its `-shm` and `-wal`), one at a time, and once after the dispatcher exits, before the archive, the step copies the store FIRST, by the online backup over a read-only connection (`snapshot.take_snapshot`, read from a private copy once the dispatcher has exited), and the rest of `out/` AFTER it: `runs/`, the lease notes (recovery state: a key whose holder died goes to nobody until its resource is shown gone, C19), the logs and the lock file, everything but the live store's own files. An attempt's row is 'running' before its run starts and records the result only after the run's record and marker are written (C04), so every attempt the store copy holds began before the backup and one it holds as ended had its marker written before it: the snapshot's store never names a record its `runs/` lacks, and `take` checks it (every ended attempt's marker present). Copied the other way, an attempt that ended between the copies is ended in the store and missing from `runs/`, and recovery, which visits only running rows, never runs it again. A run that ends after the backup is running in the copy with its marker perhaps beside it: the resume's recovery commits it, or runs it again. A run directory no row of the copy names (an attempt begun after the backup) is left out: the restored store's sequence is the copy's, so the run's next attempt gets that directory and that attempt id, and a marker left there would read as its own. Each snapshot is written into a fresh `.snap-<n>.part`, every file hashed as copied and read back, its manifest last, then renamed to `snap-<n>`; only then are older ones removed, the newest two kept. **Resume:** `step --resume` restores the newest snapshot that verifies (every file against the manifest, nothing added or missing, the store's integrity; a manifest that is no object, or a file that cannot be read, fails it the same way), passing over a damaged one and naming it, unless the archive is newer: a snapshot names the archive its step started from (`base`), and one that does not name the current archive was taken before it. A snapshot of another shard is refused, and so is a shard none of whose snapshots verifies that has no archive, rather than started afresh with its results dropped. A restored lease directory is marked rolled back (`lease.mark_rolled_back`): a note the snapshot shows released may predate a holder that took the key after it, and a key first taken after it has no file, so every note that says released names no holder and every lock file created there later starts so, and each key goes to a new holder only once its resource is shown gone (C19); the start's sweep looks at every key of the plan's groups there, those with no file too, so a key first taken after the copy counts against every unit's cap until its proof, though no run asks for it. The restored store's attempt directories, and an archive's (absolute paths of the node that ran it, which #314's resume left as they were, so recovery found no marker), are pointed at the new copy's `runs/` (`snapshot.rebase_run_dirs`). Then `expcore run` checks the store's fingerprint, shard and identity as at any resume. A start without `--resume` of a shard with a snapshot is refused. **Spend:** the core charges an attempt the copy holds as running at its bound once recovery interrupts it, and by its own journal's call log once the start has counted that journal (C05), but the attempts begun after the snapshot, their top-ups and the probe sends of a start that died before its first snapshot have no row anywhere, and nothing of the run was on the shared filesystem. So `expcore run --spend-journal FILE` journals every change of what was spent, written after the store's transaction commits (a rolled-back one writes nothing) and fsynced before the spend: an attempt's row at its begin, each top-up and its end, each probe charge with its event, and each model taken off the reply maximum (C27), which raises the bound its attempts are charged. A write that fails raises before the run starts or the send is made, and every later one too, so the dispatcher stops. Every line carries the campaign's fingerprint hash, kept from the first start that journaled (a grown plan records a new fingerprint), and the shard's whole spec as its store pins it (rule, index, count, label: the same label in another partition is another shard). The next start counts every journal of the directory against the store before its probes, once (the store's ledger names each journal counted): of the store's own journal, the attempts, top-ups past the row's and probe charges the store lacks; of a journal it has not counted, everything, since a start counts every journal before it writes its own; and a model any of them took off the reply maximum is taken off it in the store, in the same transaction, so the attempts the store holds are charged by the window too. A journal of another campaign or shard, a damaged line before the last, or a store whose own journal is missing refuses the start, naming it. A lost attempt is charged as its row would be (when its end was not journaled, by its journaled call log, else its reserved bound, C05; else its measured cost or its bound), a top-up or probe charge at its dollars, against the shard's share and so the campaign's total, by the gate and by `selection_ledger.spend_of`; the export lists them (`lost_in_kill`), a merged export with each shard, and the report has a section "Spend lost in kills". *Learned:* #314 (M5) left the hook (`Snapshots`) and the coordinator's three requirements (16 Nov): a consistent order, atomic snapshots, spend never lost. Tracing the attempt's life (`attempt._begin`, `_finish`, `recovery.recover`) showed runs-first breaks the stated guarantee and store-first keeps it; `begin_attempt`'s `tries = attempts + 1` with `mkdir(exist_ok=True)` and the AUTOINCREMENT sequence carried by the backup showed why an orphan directory must go; and the run's log the requirement named did not exist on the shared filesystem (the whole of `out/` is on tmpfs), so the journal is it. The Codex review of the branch found a reply-cap revocation after the snapshot lost with it (the copy's attempts charged the smaller bound), journals told apart by label alone (a partition of another count kept the label), a manifest that is JSON but no object ending the resume instead of falling back, and a shard whose every snapshot failed started afresh; the lease notes, which are recovery state, were also left out of the first snapshots. Its delta review then found that the notes copied back can show a key released that a holder took after the snapshot, and give a key first taken after it no note, so both went to the next run without the proof; and its next delta review, that the start's sweep skipped a key with no file, so a sandbox left on it was in no unit's count. **Not run live:** a step killed by walltime or a node's end on the site, snapshots on its Lustre (whose fsync costs and read-back cache are untested), and a resume on another node. *Test:* a snapshot restores the store and the rest of `out/` byte for byte with directories under the new `out/`, the lease notes, the logs and the lock file included and the live store's own files not; a manifest that is `null`, a list, a string or an object without its keys fails the restore; a run that ends between the store's backup and the copy of `runs/` is running in the snapshot with its marker, and recovery commits it; runs copied first leave a done row whose marker the copy lacks; a directory begun after the backup, with a stale marker carrying the id the restored store gives the run's next attempt, is left out, and that attempt finds no marker; a kill mid-backup, mid-copy of `runs/`, after the check and as the rename is made leaves the last good snapshot the newest, its `.part` never restored and removed by the next take, and a kill after the rename leaves both; the newest two are kept; a file changed, gone or added, a manifest that cannot be read and a changed store each fail the restore and leave nothing; the real dispatcher resumes a restored snapshot, counts a lost start's attempts and runs the campaign to its end; the journal writes a line per begin, top-up and end after its commit, with the campaign and the shard, none for a refused begin or a failed finish, raises on a write that fails and on every later one, names a probe charge's event, is created new, reads a cut last line and a cut header as not written, and refuses another campaign's or shard's line and a damaged line before the last; of the store's own journal the lost attempts are charged measured or at the bound, a top-up past the row's and a probe charge the store lacks are charged, and the attempt it holds is its own; the gate's spend and `spend_of` count them against the ceiling; a journal is counted once across two resumes, and a copy taken before a count counts it once more; a journal whose store never reached the copy is lost whole, even an attempt whose id a row of the copy has; a rolled-back begin is never counted; a journal of another campaign or shard, a start as another shard, a store without its own journal and a ledger that cannot be read are refused, the last holding every model; a grown plan reads its earlier journals as its own; a lease directory marked rolled back asks the proof for a key whose note said released and for a key that had no file, grants neither until it is shown gone, and asks no more once it is; its start's sweep counts a key with no file as may-be-live until the proof, and a directory never rolled back skips such a key as before; the same label in a partition of another count is refused; a model a lost start took off the reply maximum is off it in the copy put back, and the attempt it holds and the one it lost are charged by the window; the mark is a journal line; the export lists the losses and a merged export names each shard, and the report's section shows them; on the shard driver, a step killed after a snapshot resumes from it, counts the two attempts begun after it once across a second kill and resume, and charges them against the shard's budget; a damaged newest snapshot falls back to the one before, a newest manifest of `null` or `[]` too; a shard whose every snapshot fails and that has no archive is refused, the dispatcher never started; an archive newer than every snapshot resumes with its directories pointed at the new copy, and a snapshot newer than the archive resumes; a start without `--resume` with a snapshot and a snapshot of another shard are refused; a shard that ends normally archives exactly what its dispatcher left; a store a read left with its `-shm` is settled before the archive; each step journals to a new file; a run's end takes a snapshot before the clock would, and an attempt's beginning takes none (the thread's first reading, of an `out/` with no `runs/` yet, is a count of 0, so the first attempt's beginning is no run's end; the clock's snapshot still comes); and the manifest's `snapshot_every_s` reaches the step's line.

**O63. The HPC path from `up` to `down` holds together: the agent's sandbox is declared by the pilot's template and pinned from `site.env`, the shard step makes its work root and carries its federation's platform file, and no step's directory is left on a node or lost.** Each piece of the HPC path had its own tests and fakes; wired together through the files each writes for the next (`tests/e2e/test_hpc_rehearsal.py`), they did not meet. **The agent's sandbox:** the pilot's template declared no `[sandbox]` and `up` filled no placeholder for the Apptainer pins or the work root, so no shard step could run the backend from the file `up` rendered, and the pilot's layout held no agent's node, so `shards.py plan` refused it. The template now declares the Apptainer sandbox (its limits size the agent's node, an 8-core, 17 GiB step) with placeholders `up` fills from `site.env` (`hpcpins`): the SIF and the egress shim with the SHA-256 each is declared to have, which `up` checks against the file before the cluster is touched, refusing a missing variable, a missing file or another hash and naming the variable, never the path; the bundle's own collector, hashed from the checkout; the work root. The pins reach the campaign fingerprint through `PlatformConfig.fingerprint()`'s `sandbox_backend`, and no site path does. **The work root:** nothing made it on the agent's node, and the sandbox refuses a start without it; `shards.py step` makes it from the federation's `platform.toml`, 0700, and refuses one that is a link, another user's, open to others, or not under `--tmpfs` (never the shared filesystem). **The platform file:** the step copies `<root>/platform.toml` beside the study's `study.toml` (a copy, never a link out of the study; a study holding one of its own is refused), so `study.toml`'s bytes are every shard's; every path `up` renders into the root is absolute (`@hpc_root@`), so the copy reads the root's secrets, CA and fault journal. **The node's tmpfs** is the node's, not the job's, and a step killed outright left its directory (the study's copy and its `out/`) there for good: each step records its pid, start time, shard, federation's root, results directory, the archive it started from, and its phase (`step.json`): `running` while its dispatcher runs, `archiving` from the moment it ends (swapped in by rename from a record written at the start, so a full tmpfs cannot stop it). Only a step killed while `running` is removed, once its process and every process whose argv names its directory (the dispatcher outlives a wrapper killed alone) are shown gone: by `--resume`, its own shard's of its own campaign (spec, root, results), refusing while one still runs, and by `down`, which runs `shards.py sweep` as a control step on the agent's node, its federation's. A directory whose dispatcher ended and that was not archived (marked `ARCHIVE-FAILED.json` when the mark could be written), or whose record cannot be read, is never removed: `--resume` refuses while one of its own is there, the sweep names it, and `step --archive-left` archives it with that step's exit status, only while the published archive is the one that step started from. The Codex review found the first rules unsafe three ways: a wrapper's pid alone let a surviving dispatcher's directory go, a label alone let one campaign take another's out/, and a mark that could not be written read as no mark. Its delta review found two more: a dispatcher could still end after its wrapper died, so the dispatcher now asks the kernel to kill it with its step (`PR_SET_PDEATHSIG`, kept across the exec), and `--archive-left` could take a step's directory between its dispatcher's end and its own archive, so it now refuses while the step or a process naming the directory lives; and the end's record, enriched in place, could be cut by a full tmpfs, so it is written to a file of its own, read back, and the record written at the start is used when that fails. Its second delta review found that a dispatcher ending just before its wrapper's death could still be left under a `running` record, and that the death signal failed open: the dispatcher's first words now fail closed when the signal cannot be asked (exit 71), and it runs `expcore` in its own process and swaps the step's record to `archiving` as its own last act, by any end but a kill. Its third delta review found the dispatcher's SystemExit message dropped; the exit now passes through untouched. **The study's pre-step guard and post-run script.** A study's own refusals (one worker, each planned model's cap exactly 1, a federation root of its own) must hold at every shard's start, and the step makes the work root and its tmpfs directory itself, so a check in the study's plugin runs too late and a wrapper is skipped by a line run directly. The shard manifest's `pre_step` names a script inside the study (a path relative to its root; refused when absolute, with an empty, `.` or `..` part, through a link, not a regular file, missing or over 1 MiB, at `plan` and `lines`, which print nothing), with `pre_step_timeout_s` (60 s by default, above 0, at most 600). `plan` records its SHA-256 and every line carries `--pre-step`, `--pre-step-sha256` and `--pre-step-timeout`; no line is made from an unpinned plan, and with none declared the lines and the plan are byte for byte what they were. `step` reads and hashes the declared scripts before anything is made and runs the guard first, before the core check, before a resume removes a killed step's directory and before anything is made: it holds the stamped study's root open (refused unless it is this user's and no group or other can write to it), reads the guard below it part by part with `O_NOFOLLOW`, refuses a hash that is not the plan's, and runs the bytes it hashed (never the file read again) as `python -I -B - LABEL CAMPAIGN.TOML`, with the held root as its working directory and campaign.toml named through it, so a root renamed after the check is not the one it runs in, under the step's allowlist and no-home variables, in a session of its own, its whole group killed at the bound (even when its leader has just exited) or when the step is told to stop. A non-zero exit, a signal, a timeout, a mismatch or an unreadable guard refuses the step with status 73, naming the guard, with no work root, step directory, journal or archive made; a pass is recorded as `pre_step` (path, hash, exit status) in `step.json` and the archive's manifest. **The post-run script** (`post_run`, `post_run_timeout_s`: 300 s by default, at most 1800) is the same shape for the check that needs the live federation: it runs once the dispatcher has exited 0 and before `archive()`, as `python -I -B - LABEL OUT STATUS` in the study copy's root, in the dispatcher's environment, and its exit 0 makes the shard's results final. A non-zero exit, a signal, a timeout, or a dispatcher that did not exit 0 (the script is not run) archives the results all the same, so nothing is lost, with `final: false` and the reason in the manifest and `NOT-FINAL.json` in `out/`, and the step ends with status 74 (the dispatcher's own when it was not 0); `collect` refuses a shard that is not final naming it and the reason, and a shard whose manifest does not say the plan's post-run script passed (a line that dropped it), and the core's `merge` refuses an `out/` holding the mark, naming the shard; `--resume` starts from such an archive without the mark; `--archive-left` archives final only what the step's record says passed, and not final otherwise. **The data each space holds** (C06): a campaign's shards on several federations are one campaign only where each space declares its data, so every template's `[[spaces]]` entry names `data_manifest = "@data_manifest.<space>@"`: a shared import's is the manifest its layout already names (its variable), every other space's is written by `up` into the root from its baseline (`hpcdata`, deterministic, so one layout's federations agree). A pin is never taken on a claim: `verify_imports` shows each shared import holds its manifest, and `up` reads every other space back through its owner (every directory, every file's size and bytes) and fails, naming the space and never a path, when it is not exactly its manifest, before the link check. `shards.py plan` refuses a manifest of several federations whose platform files leave a space's data undeclared, naming each shard and space, and any federation whose `up` has not finished (its state's phase). **A step killed outright, seen live (slot 5).** `down`'s sweep left the killed run's sandbox (its directory and `_runs` record under the work root, and a dead `fuse.oneclient` mount, "Transport endpoint is not connected"), an empty work root stayed on every node a shard step ran on, and the resume on another node ran from a line edited by hand that nothing checked (W1, W2). `down`'s sweep, and the next shard step on a node before it uses its work root, now run the Apptainer run reaper over the work root (each dead run's mounts unmounted, `fusermount3 -u` then `-uz`, shown gone by the mount table, then its directory and record), a dead run it cannot clear failing the sweep and refusing the step; the sweep removes the work root by `rmdir` once it is empty and no step directory is on the node, runs under the core's Python (`HPC_CORE_PYTHON`, which `up` asks for with an agent's node), and fails closed under one without the core. `shards.py resume-line --manifest M --label L --job J --node N` prints the shard's line with `--resume` in the operator's job J on N only when J is RUNNING, this user's, of one component and the one node N, no services' job, with the manifest's cores and memory free (its running steps' `AllocTRES`); no step of the shard is listed under any job of this user's and its running mark names no step still listed; and the newest snapshot that verifies (now with every ended attempt's marker, `out_snapshot.restore`) names the shard and its archive and pins the fingerprint the study pins with this federation's `platform.toml` (`pipeline.campaign_fingerprint`, by the manifest's Python), as every other shard does; it records J and N in the state (`shard_nodes`), so `down` sweeps N in J, which it never cancels (one ended is said). Every step keeps the shard's running mark (`<results>/<label>/running.json`: node, process, job, step name, nonce) from its start to its own end, written exclusively: a start is refused while one is there, and a `--resume` takes one over only on the node it names once its process is gone, or with the nonce resume-line showed gone (`--over`), by first making that mark's one claim exclusively (never removed) and then renaming its own over it, so the mark is never absent and of any number of launches one alone runs; `down` cancels a shard step of the federation still running in a resume's job by its id before it sweeps that node, and fails that sweep while one is still there. **Not run live:** `resume-line` on the site, `sacct`'s step lines as its accounting prints them, and a reap of a real dead FUSE mount by the sweep. *Learned:* the offline rehearsal of the HPC path (16 Nov 2026), before the first live slot; the coordinator's decisions on each seam. Two fakes were made to say what the next stage needs: `FakeSlurm`'s zone exported no certificate (`ca_pem`), and `FakeNode`'s driver was one invented process alive on every fake node (`own_process`), so a killed dispatcher's run record would never have been reaped. **Not run live:** the whole path on the site; the sweep on a node's real tmpfs. *Test:* `up` hands the core the site's declared pins, paths and work root and the bundle's collector, and the rendered file loads as the Apptainer backend on them; a variable missing, a file missing and a file of another hash each refuse `up` before any submit, naming the variable and never the path; a template naming no pin needs none; two renders differing in one pin differ in the platform's fingerprint by that pin alone, and the fingerprint holds no site value but the two pins; a copy of the rendered file elsewhere reads the root's secrets, CA and journal; the step makes the work root 0700 before the dispatcher, takes a correct one as it is, and refuses a link, one open to others, one off `--tmpfs`, another user's and one with no parent; without the Apptainer sandbox it makes none; each step's copy of the study holds its federation's platform file as a regular 0600 file beside an unchanged `study.toml`, and a study holding one is refused; the step's directory records its process, shard and root; a resume removes its shard's gone directories and no other's, keeps one naming no step and one of another federation or campaign under the same label, refuses while one of its shard runs, and while a process naming the directory outlives its step; a step whose dispatcher ended keeps its out/ whether or not its mark could be written, and one whose record cannot be read is kept; an `out/` that could not be archived is recorded with its exit status, refuses the resume, is archived by `--archive-left` (only this campaign's, never over a later archive) and the resume then runs from it; the sweep removes its federation's killed steps' directories and names each one kept; a dispatcher dies with its step killed alone; `--archive-left` refuses a live step's directory; a full tmpfs at the dispatcher's end still leaves a whole `archiving` record; the dispatcher records its own end before it exits; one that cannot be tied to its step never starts; the dispatcher's exit status, stdout and stderr are `python -I -m expcore`'s own; `down` runs it on the agent's node in its component or its own job, and a sweep that fails keeps the root but never the job billing; the data manifests `up` renders are each space's baseline (an empty one for a space with none) or a shared import's own; two federations whose ids differ give one platform fingerprint; a federation one of whose files differs by one byte fails `up` naming the space, with no link record; `plan` refuses several federations with a space's data undeclared, in either form, takes them when every space declares it, asks nothing of one federation, and refuses a federation whose `up` did not finish; and the rehearsal runs every stage, both shards recording one campaign fingerprint with each space's data identity and the sandbox's pins and no site value but the pins, a shard on a third federation declaring another build of the agent's image refused at the merge by that pin alone, the killed step's directory removed by the resume and nothing of a step left after `down`; the sweep reaping a killed run's dead mount (by a plain unmount, and by a lazy one), its directory and record, and removing the empty work root, failing with an unreadable mount table, under a Python without the core and on a link, and keeping the work root of a live run or while a step directory is on the node; the next step reaping a killed run before its dispatcher, and refusing when it cannot; the running mark held while a step runs and gone at its end, a start or an unproven resume refused by one of another node, one taken over with its nonce or on its own node once its process is gone, and the second of two takeovers refused; `resume-line`'s line (the job, the node, `/`, the step's name, `--resume`, `--over`) and its record, once, and its refusals (a step of the shard listed, a marked step listed, a mark naming no step, the services' job, another node, an unknown job, another user's or a pending one, cores held by its steps or an accounting it cannot read, no snapshot, a snapshot of another campaign, another shard pinning another, an unlinked federation, an unreadable manifest); `down` sweeping the resume node in its job by the core's Python and never cancelling it, saying one that ended, and keeping the root when that sweep fails; a step into an unrecorded job refused; malformed `shard_nodes` refused; `up` refusing an agent's node without `HPC_CORE_PYTHON`; a takeover of a mark another step already claimed refused with the mark in place, and a start meanwhile refused; `down` cancelling the shard's step still running in the resume job, and no other step of it, before its sweep there, which fails while a step of the federation still runs; and a snapshot whose manifest leaves out an ended attempt's marker never restored; and a guard that exits 0 lets the step proceed and is recorded, one that exits non-zero, dies by a signal, overruns its bound or is not the file the plan hashed (even if the file is swapped after the check) refuses the step with nothing made (the work root, the step's directory, the journals and the archive absent, and a resume's earlier directory kept), a guard path that is absolute, climbs or leads through a link refuses the plan and the step, a study root that is not this user's or that others can write refuses the step, a root renamed after its check is not the one the guard runs in, the guard runs with `-I -B` in the study's root under the allowlist alone with the label and campaign path, its whole group is killed at the bound even when its leader has just exited and when the step is told to stop, and with no script declared the line and the step are unchanged; a post-run script that is not as declared refuses the step before the dispatcher, runs after a dispatcher that exited 0 and before the archive in the copy's root with the label, `out/` and the status, makes the results final on exit 0, and otherwise archives them not final (manifest, the mark in the archive's `out/`, written into the tar itself so a full tmpfs cannot leave it out, exit 74; whatever the script or the store's settling does, the results are archived), a dispatcher that did not exit 0 archives them not final without running it, `collect` and the core's merge refuse a not-final shard naming it, `--resume` starts from one afresh and `--archive-left` archives final only what its step's record says passed. **The study's own paths on the nodes come from `site.env`.** A platform template may name `@study_artifacts@` (its tool server's venv, clone and manifests), which `up` fills from `HPC_STUDY_ARTIFACTS` (an absolute path of plain characters with no `..`, on a filesystem the login node and the agent's node see), so a public replication package carries no site path; a template or a space's `data_manifest` that uses the placeholder without the key is refused before any job, naming the variable, and an unset key is no value, so the renderer refuses the placeholder rather than write an empty path. **A shard's archive is unpacked only as an `out/`.** `collect` and `--resume` extract an archive with `shards.unpack`, which refuses the whole archive, before anything is made or extracted, when a member's name has a `..` component or is not `out` or under `out/`, when a link's target is absolute, has a `..` after a name or lands outside `out/` (a hard link's from the archive's root), or when a member's path passes through a link of the archive; `tarfile`'s data filter alone judges a member against the directory unpacked into, and let `out/../x`, a link to `../x` and a member written through a link land beside `out/`, outside the tree the merge reads. The refusal names the shard and the kind of problem, never a member's name or target. *Test:* the placeholder rendered from the key; the core's phase handed it and the state recording it; the refusal without the key for the template and for a space's manifest; the unset key giving the renderer no value; the key refused when relative, with `..`, a space, a quote, a backslash, a semicolon or a newline, naming the variable and never the value; a site without the key unchanged; an archive with `out/../x`, `out/../out/x`, an absolute name, a name outside `out/`, a symbolic or hard link leaving `out/` (absolute, or with a `..` after a name) and a member written through a link, each refused whole with nothing made, naming the shard and no member of it, and through `collect`; a normal archive, with links that stay inside `out/`, unpacked as before. **The shard archives' copy back, and what lies beside them:** nothing durable may live only on a cluster's scratch (a site's scratch policy can remove files 30 days after creation, a job directory 7 days after its job and all of scratch after 20 days with no job submitted), and the cluster cannot push to the operator's host, so the operator pulls the results directory after `shards.py collect` and before `down`, and the core makes the pull checkable. `collect` writes `archives.sha256.json` beside the archives (`expcore-hpc-archives/1`: a `plan_id`, the SHA-256 of the plan's study and results directories and each shard's index, count, label and root, and each archive's relative name, size and SHA-256, from the private copy it verified and never from a step's claim; then `evidence`: each shard's `<label>.out.json`, every file under its `<label>/journals/` (a step killed outright spent what only its journal holds, which `--resume` counts as `lost_in_kill`) and, only when newer than the archive, its newest verified snapshot, each by relative name, size and SHA-256 of the bytes read; the same bytes for every collect of unchanged results, none from a refused collect) and prints that file's SHA-256 on stderr after the merge line. `shards.py verify-copy MANIFEST LOCAL_DIR --manifest-sha256 HASH` (standard library only, run before the plan is loaded, so it needs no core) refuses a manifest that is not the one hashed, that names no archive or no evidence file (an earlier tip's) or that names anything but a plain file name or a relative path of plain parts (exit 2), names every archive or evidence file that is missing, not a regular file, under a link, of another size or of another SHA-256 (exit 1: names and reasons, never a path), and only warns of an archive, archive manifest or journal it does not name. **A snapshot is evidence only when newer than the archive:** a snapshot names the archive its step started from, `--resume` restores one only if it names the shard's current archive, so the snapshots of the step that wrote an archive hold nothing it lacks, and those of a step resumed from it and killed hold the runs it lacks; `collect` lists the newest of those that verifies (the pick `--resume` makes) and warns, and `collect --run` refuses before any merge (exit 2, naming the shard and the snapshot; no manifest, no directory left), since `merge` has no flag to leave a shard short. *Test:* `collect` writes the manifest of the archives it read, with the size of the bytes read and not a step's claim, the same bytes every time and none when it refuses, and prints its hash apart from the merge line; the plan id tells plans of the same labels apart; `verify-copy` passes an exact copy and fails a missing archive, one changed byte, a truncated archive, a directory or a link of an archive's name and every failure of one pull in one run, refuses a manifest that is not the printed one or that it cannot read as one (a name outside the directory, no archive, a size or hash missing) and a hash that is not a SHA-256, warns of an extra archive without failing, and does all of it on an interpreter that cannot import the core; the manifest lists each shard's archive manifest and journals (files in subdirectories too) with the hashes of the bytes read and refuses the collect whole for a link among them or an archive manifest that no longer names the verified archive; a step that ended lists none of its snapshots and a step resumed from the archive and killed (through `shards.step`) lists its newest, with a warning (plain `collect` exits 0 and prints the merge line; `collect --run` exits 2 naming the shard and the snapshot, runs no merge, and names every shard in that state; with no snapshot ahead of its archive it still runs the merge), the newest that verifies when two name the archive and the older when the newest is damaged, and refuses one of another shard as `--resume` does; `verify-copy` fails a missing, truncated or changed journal, archive manifest or snapshot file, a journal that is a directory or a link, and a `journals/` that is a link to a directory of the same files, names every failure of a pull in one run, warns of an extra journal or archive manifest, and refuses an evidence list that is missing, empty, or has a name leaving the directory, an entry without size or hash or a name given twice. **The REST arm's 443 mapper (#384).** A REST level on an HPC federation needs a content check file, a REST data-path level and the connect-side 443 mapper as a pinned preload, and the pilot's template declared none, so a probe rendered a private copy. `layouts/pilot-shape-rest.toml` is the pilot's layout but for its own template, which adds those three and nothing else; the pilot's template is unchanged and names no mapper. The mapper and its SHA-256 come from two optional `site.env` keys (`HPC_GATEPORT`, `HPC_GATEPORT_SHA256`) as the placeholders `@hpc_gateport@` and `@hpc_gateport_sha@`, and `up`'s pin check (`hpcpins.check`) refuses, before anything is submitted and naming the variable, a template that names them without the keys, a missing mapper and one with another SHA-256; the rendered file's preload carries the declared SHA-256, which the sandbox checks at every start and the campaign fingerprint holds, never the path. *Test:* the layout equal to the pilot's but for its template, and the REST template equal to the pilot's but for the three declarations; the check file one the layout's own baseline writes in the study space, the first `[[spaces]]`; the placeholders filled from the keys and handed to the core's phase; a key set to a relative path, a path with `..` or a malformed hash refused; the state file keeping both values and an older one loading; each key missing, the file missing and the file changed refusing before any submit with no state written and no path in the message; the pilot's layout reading no mapper though the site declares one; the rendered file loading both levels, the check file and the preload's path and pin; two renders differing in the mapper's pin differing in the fingerprint's `preload_sha256` alone. **One campaign fingerprint before any line.** The campaign fingerprint holds the platform settings that shape a run, which each federation's `platform.toml` renders (the faults sanction and targets, the placement bounds, the data identity). Shards on federations that rendered different ones pinned different fingerprints, and `merge` refused them only after the shards had run and the money was spent; `plan` and `lines` printed a line for each. They now take each shard's campaign fingerprint first, by the function `resume-line` uses (`shardresume.study_fingerprint`: the manifest's Python, `-I -B`, on a private copy of the study with that federation's `platform.toml` beside it), and print nothing unless every shard's is the same; `fleet.py plan` does the same for the shards it assigns, before it assigns or writes anything, which its platform-fingerprint check (the platform's half alone) left to the merge for a setting only the study fingerprints. The refusal names each shard that differs from the largest group of equal shards by its label and the first differing top-level key of the fingerprint, never a host, a path or a value; a `platform.toml` that cannot be read, a study that cannot be copied and a program that ends non-zero or is missing refuse by the shard's label, with the program's status and none of its output. *Test:* three federations that render different spaces and the same settings print every line, each fingerprint taken beside its own federation's file; one that renders another `[faults]` or `[placement]` (or none) refuses `plan` and `lines` with nothing printed, naming the shard, or the shards that differ from the largest group, and `platform`, with no path and no value; a key only one federation's fingerprint has is named; a missing or non-file `platform.toml`, a study gone, a program that ends non-zero (its words never repeated) and one that is missing refuse by label; the real interpreter on a real study takes one fingerprint of three federations; the fleet's plan refuses a federation whose study fingerprint differs though its platform's does not, assigning and writing nothing and launching nothing, and does not read the file of a shard no federation holds. *Not run live:* the check on the site's interpreter and a real study with a platform half. **The operator's own variables reach the dispatcher, pinned by the line.** A step's environment is an allowlist, so the operator's half of a study's fault sanction, a variable in the dispatcher's environment, never arrived and every fault-injecting run launched through `shards.py` was refused. The manifest's `pass_env` names such variables (`^[A-Z][A-Z0-9_]{0,63}$`; refused when the name contains KEY, TOKEN, SECRET, PASSWORD, PASSWD, CREDENTIAL or AUTH, or is one the step sets itself: its allowlist, `HOME`, `TMPDIR`, its home's XDG, Apptainer, uv, pip and git names, anything of `EXPCORE_` or `PYTHON`). `plan`, `lines` and `resume-line` read each value from the operator's environment before anything is printed or recorded (`pin_pass_env`; never through `pinned`, which `collect` calls), refusing an unset variable or a value that is neither a plain word of `[A-Za-z0-9._:=,+-]` (at most 128, and not `.` or `..`) nor an absolute path of `[A-Za-z0-9._/-]` (at most 1024 after its slash, no `..` or `.` part, no `//`: the pilot's study artifacts directory), naming the variable and never the value; each line carries the value as an `env -i` word `NAME=VALUE` and the declaration as `--pass-env NAME`, so a step cannot gain or change one. `step` checks again as its first act (status 73, nothing made), passes the dispatcher the allowlist, its home, `TMPDIR` and exactly the declared variables (the post-run script's environment is the dispatcher's; the guard's is not), and records each name and value in `step.json` and the archive's manifest, which `--archive-left` carries. `step` never reads the manifest, so a line that dropped a declaration with its word is seen at `collect`, which refuses a shard whose manifest lacks a declared name or holds an undeclared one, naming the shard and the variable. **Not run live:** a fault-injecting run on a federation with the variable. *Test:* a declared name is pinned on the printed line with its value (and `plan` shows it), the line otherwise byte for byte what it was; the value on the node is the line's, not the operator's later one, for an empty value, one of every allowed character, one of 128 and absolute paths (one of 1024 after its slash); a name that looks like a secret, one the step sets itself (every name of its home too), a name that is not plain upper case, a `pass_env` that is not a list of distinct names, an unset variable (the second of two refusing the first's line too) and a value with a space, quote, `$`, newline, backslash, non-ASCII or past 128 characters, a relative path, `.` or `..`, a path with a `..` or `.` part, a `//`, a `;` or a `:`, a bare `/` and one past 1024 each refuse `plan` and `lines` with nothing printed, the value never in the message; through the real `step`, as the printed line runs it, the declared name reaches the dispatcher with no other variable of the node's, and step.json and the archive's manifest hold it; a line that dropped its word, a bad value, a secret-looking or step-owned name and a repeated one end the step with status 73 and nothing made; the post-run script sees it and the guard does not (through the printed line, an absolute-path value beside the sanction variable reaches the dispatcher and a post-run script that exits non-zero unless it sees both, and the results are final; the same script in a step whose line declared neither leaves them not final); `--archive-left` carries it; `collect` takes shards that recorded the plan's names with no operator variable set, refuses one whose line lacked a name, one that was given a name the plan does not declare and a recorded value that is not a value, and takes an older archive with no record under a plan declaring none; a sanction stand-in that is a child process refuses to start (status 3) under the line of a manifest without the key and runs under the line of one declaring it, and `collect` takes both shards; no line is made from a plan whose values were not read; `resume-line` pins the variable and refuses an unset one before it records its node. **The fleet and `resume-line` carry the study's own refusals and the operator's variables.** `resume-line` and `fleetplan.line_of` made their lines from a plan nothing had hashed, so `step_argv` refused a manifest that declared `pre_step`, `post_run` or `expect_render_identity` (the shard could not be resumed; the fleet escaped with a traceback), and the fleet's shard settings took only `snapshot_every_s`, so no step it launched could run the study's guard. Both now pin as `plan` does (`pinned`, then `pin_pass_env`), and the settings take the rest of the manifest's optional keys and `pass_env`, checked by the manifest's own rules. `fleet.py plan` and `step` read each declared variable from the environment they run in on every line they print or launch (an unset one or a bad value refuses the whole command before anything is assigned or launched; a line pins what the operator holds when `step` runs, unless the shard's first step recorded its values: see the next paragraph), `plan` prints the values beside each line, and `shards.toml` declares the names, so `collect` refuses a shard whose step was not given them. *Test:* a manifest declaring a guard and a post-run script gives a resume line and a fleet line carrying each path and its SHA-256, and a missing script refuses `resume-line` before it records its node and the fleet's `plan` before it assigns a shard; the fleet's `plan` and `step` pin the guard and the post-run script; `pass_env` in the fleet's settings is pinned on every line `plan` prints (with the values) and `step` launches, from the environment each runs in, on a relaunch with `--resume` too, declared in shards.toml; a secret-looking name, one the step sets itself, a bad one, a string and a repeat, an unset variable (at `plan`, and gone at `step`: nothing launched) and a value that is no plain word or path (never echoed) each refuse. **A shard's declared variables keep one value for its whole life.** Each launch pinned what the operator held when it ran, so a resume (`step --resume`, `resume-line`) or a later fleet step could carry another value than the shard's first step had, or add or drop a variable: a shard whose first step ran without the fault sanction could finish with it, or the reverse. The first step that passes variables now records each name and value once, in `<results>/<label>/pass-env.json` (`record_pass_env_origin`: a draft of its own and an exclusive link, mode 0600, once the step holds the shard's running mark, so two first steps agree or one is refused; nothing is written when it passes none), and every later step compares with it before anything is made or launched (`check_pass_env_origin`): `step` as its first act (status 73), whether or not it is a `--resume`, since a first step killed before its first snapshot leaves this record and no archive; `resume-line` before it asks Slurm anything or records its node; the fleet's `step` for each shard it would launch, refusing the whole command (`plan` and `lines` print first steps' lines and are not compared). A variable whose value differs, one the first step did not pass and one it passed that the line dropped are refused naming the variable, never either value; a record that is empty, not JSON, another shard's, a link, too long or holding a name or value a variable may not carry is refused, never taken for none; a shard with results (an archive or a snapshot) and no record had a first step that passed nothing, so a step that now passes a declared variable adds one mid-shard and is refused the same way, naming the variable; with neither a record nor results nothing is compared. **Not covered:** a first step that passed nothing and left no archive or snapshot (killed before its first snapshot) leaves nothing to compare, so a later step may then pass variables and record them as the shard's. *Test:* a first step records its values (0600, no draft left), a step that passes nothing or is refused before it runs records none; through the real `step`, a `--resume` with a changed value is refused (73) naming the variable with neither value in the message, its dispatcher never started, the archive and the record untouched, and the same values proceed with the record untouched; a plain relaunch of a shard whose first step left only its record is compared too; a later step that adds or drops a name is refused naming it (and not the one that agrees); an empty, non-JSON, non-record, other shard's, mistyped or linked record refuses the step; a label the core refuses reaches its own refusal; `resume-line` refuses a changed value, a dropped variable and an unreadable record before it asks Slurm anything and with the federation's state untouched, and prints its line for the same values; the fleet's `step --resume` refuses a changed value whole, naming only the variable and launching nothing, launches with the same values, compares each shard with its own record, takes a first launch and leaves `plan` as it was; a variable added to a shard that has results and no record is refused (through the real `step` after a first step that passed nothing and archived, with a snapshot alone, by `resume-line` and by the fleet's `step`, and by the writer when the results appeared after the first check), and a resume that passes nothing after a first step that passed nothing is what it was. **The root's evidence and the atomic collect (G9):** `down` removes the federation's root, which holds what the study's post-check and the facts read, and `collect` did not archive it; and `collect` wrote `archives.sha256.json` before its unpack loop, so a refusal inside the unpack exited 2 with the manifest on scratch, no hash printed and the earlier trees already in `--into`. `collect` now archives each federation's root (`hpcevidence.py`) to `<results>/<label>.root.tar` with `<label>.root.json`, from an allow-list of files read once below a descriptor of the root with no link followed (`hpc-federation.json`, `platform.toml`, `facts.json`, `link-check.json`, `render-identity.json`, `day0-wiring.toml`, `shard-claim.json` and `.lock`, `data-manifests/*.json`, `shard-steps/*.json`, the whole `fault-journal/` directory, the fault relay's `late-steps.jsonl` and its request, taken and answer files), never `secrets/`, a file that holds a token, key or password, or the relay's heartbeat and lock; a file holding the content of a file of `secrets/` refuses the collect, naming the file and never the value; the state and the facts are required, and a root that is gone is shown by an earlier verified root archive or the collect is refused. Both are listed with their hashes in `archives.sha256.json`'s evidence and checked by `verify-copy` like the journals. `shards.py step` leaves `shard-steps/<label>.json` in its root (a step ran there; one that cannot write it does not start), `collect` writes `evidence-collected.json` there naming the root archive's hash and the digest of those records, and `down` refuses, before it stops anything, a root with step records and no current marker (or one where a file a step or the study writes (the journals, the relay's files, the claims, the step records, the manifests, what `up` wrote once) is no longer what the marker names, compared file by file: a later step, a step still writing, a changed file; the state and the link check, which the running federation rewrites itself, are no reason to refuse, and `down` names them in its output and in the state's `evidence_rewritten_after_collect`, the collect-time copy staying what the post-check reads), unless `--discard-evidence`, which its output and the state file record (time, shards, `--discard-reason`); a root where no step ran goes as before. `collect` stages every shard in a private directory beside `--into`, checks it all, and only then renames the trees into place, publishes the root archives and writes the manifest (a draft, fsynced, renamed) last, printing its hash, then writes the markers: a refused collect leaves `--into` as it found it and writes no manifest, no root archive and no marker. `<label>/pass-env.json` (the shard's first-step `pass_env`) is listed in the evidence when it is there, and checked by `verify-copy` like a journal. The root archive, its manifest and `pass-env.json` are private (node names, site paths, the account's details): `expcore package` never selects a `*.root.tar`, `*.root.json` or `pass-env.json` (`package.spec.never_selected`), whatever directory they lie in and whatever the patterns say. A shard that moves to another federation keeps its first federation's root archive: the manifest records a hash of the root's path (`root_id`) and of the allocation's name (`up_id`: a federation brought up again at the same root is another), the next collect puts an archive of another root or `up` aside (`<label>.<12 hex digits>.root.*`, listed and checked) and never overwrites it, and `collect --roots-only [--shard L]` archives and marks the roots alone for a shard about to move. The secret scan fails closed (an unreadable `secrets/` refuses the collect) and the archives are mode 0600. **A plugin that needs a declared variable is fingerprinted at plan.** The program that builds the study's plugin to take each shard's campaign fingerprint (`shardresume.study_fingerprint`, for `plan`, `lines`, `resume-line` and the fleet's `plan`) ran with the step's allowlist and none of the declared `pass_env` variables, so a plugin that refuses to be built without one (the first study's refuses without its artifacts directory) could never be fingerprinted and no line could be printed. It is now given each declared variable with the value the lines pin (`pinned_pass_env`, the one `step_argv` reads: read and checked by `pin_pass_env` before any program runs, the fleet's from the environment it runs in) and nothing else of the operator's environment; a plan that declares variables and has not pinned them is refused before anything is copied or run. The core-identity probe builds no plugin and is given none. *Test:* a stand-in plugin that refuses to be built without two declared variables is fingerprinted by `plan`, `lines`, `resume-line` and the fleet's `plan` once they are declared and set, with the values the printed lines carry, and a real stamped study whose plugin raises without one is built by the real interpreter only when the manifest declares it; a variable the manifest does not declare (one that looks like a secret too, and the site's) is in nothing any call received; an unset declared variable refuses `plan`, `lines`, `resume-line` and the fleet's `plan`, naming it and showing no value, before the program is run; a plan that declares variables and was never pinned is refused before the program runs. *Not run live:* the first study's own plugin on the site's interpreter.

**O64. A test selection run on the HPC allocation tests the shipped commit, in a step of an allocation of ours behind the environment allowlist, writes only to the node's tmpfs, and comes back checked; nothing the runner does reaches another job.** The development host is CPU-bound and shared with another study's timed runs, so lanes run their test selections on a standing Slurm allocation through `deploy/hpc-tests/hpctests.py` (its README). **The step:** one `srun --jobid=<ours> --exact` with its own cores, memory and time limit, never `--overlap` (on a site where steps used it, every one landed on the same cores), `--export=PATH` and `/usr/bin/env -i` with PATH, the locale and TMPDIR as its whole environment; nothing of `site.env` but the job id, the node's tmpfs root and the step's PATH is on its command line.
